# Spec tracker (2026-10-25)

One row per spec. Three separate questions, because they diverge: **Approved** (the operator ratified the text), **Built** (code exists), **Proven** (a person or a live service has exercised it, not only tests and mocks). Sources: [`roadmap.md`](roadmap.md), [`roadmap-review-2026-10-24.md`](roadmap-review-2026-10-24.md), [`console-ink-handoff.md`](console-ink-handoff.md), [`decisions.md`](decisions.md). Update this file in the same PR that changes a spec's state.

**Number collisions (fixed 2026-11-08, spec 090).** Three numbers had been used twice. One spec of each pair was renumbered: [Documents and data visualization](../../specs/095-documents-and-dataviz.md) was 014 (the relay spec, [Relay and away mode](../../specs/014-relay-and-away-mode.md), keeps 014; `hub.rs`, `totp.rs`, GOVERNANCE §5.10 and REVIEW REV-SEC-09 cite it); [Console deck change log](../../specs/096-console-deck-change-log.md) was 037 (the training spec, [Training experiments](../../specs/037-training-experiments-quality-and-efficiency.md), keeps 037, because its research truth packs are fingerprinted and cite it); [Encyclopedia search pages](../../specs/097-encyclopedia-search-pages.md) was 061 (the shell-tool spec keeps 061). A citation of 014, 037 or 061 written before this date may mean either spec of its pair. `delphi lint` now refuses two spec files with one number. New specs take the next free number.

## Training experiments: quality and efficiency (spec 037, approved, 2026-10-26)

Asked for by the operator on 2026-10-26; pilot budget **80 engineering hours** (operator review time and model dollars are separate; the dollar cap OD-T1 is open). Suite: C1-C3 from repo history, R1-R6 research and strategy with stored truth packs.

| :-- | :-- | --: | :-- |
| X1 | spec 037; research case format (`truth/`, `fact_recall`, `no_traps`, `text_matches`, draft-pack rule, `train seal`); truth packs R1-R3 (drafts, operator verification pending) | 15 | merged (#215); R5, R6 drafts added (R4 not built) |
| M3 | seeded-defect review case (build plan 2026-11-07): `delphi train defects gen\|run`, `review_findings` check, report section (recall, false positives, unique catches; diagnostics only, specs/078) | 6 to 8 | built (#564); live runs 2026-11-08 hit a ceiling (recall 0.95 to 1.0); M3b adds `--difficulty easy\|medium\|hard` (24 defects, 7 subtler kinds incl. cross-function, 24 decoy twins, recall by kind in the report; easy unchanged); no live run of hard |
| X2 | per-arm template home and staffing check (`--arms-file`); tokens per model in rows | 8 | merged with X3 (#217) |
| X3 | `delphi train harvest` and `check-hidden`; code cases C1-C3 (validated: fail on base, pass 3x on reference) | 20 | merged (#217) |
| X4 | blinded bundles, jury runner (`train judge`) | 12 | built (PR open) |
| X5 | operator review CLI, disagreement ledger (`train review|disputes|resolve`) | 8 | built (PR open) |
| X6 | report (`train report`): pass rate, cost per pass, frontier, judge breakdown | 6 | built (PR open) |
| X7 | pilot supervision and debugging | 11 | not started |
| X8 | an arm may bring an endpoint the home lacks (`[arm.endpoint.<id>]`: family and harness), throwaway copy only, so one Copilot login runs as several vendors' models; `delphi train fidelity gen --inline` puts the report in the prompt for tool-less seats | 3 | built 2026-11-07 (PR pending): `train_arms.rs` (`ExtraEndpoint`), `train_fidelity.rs`. Live: Copilot catalogue read over ACP (24 models); see the PR for the first results |

R4-R6 truth packs join in the first study phase.

Note 2026-11-08: `train defects gen --difficulty hard --inline` (and `train fidelity gen --inline` above 40 items) overran the 8,000-byte task envelope. Above 6000 bytes `--inline` now writes the artifact to `fixture/` and the prompt points at it (`@project/repo/...`); a seat with no tools gets it as a slice, so tool-less seats can take the hard case. The limit itself is unchanged.

## Central error log (spec 039, approved, 2026-10-27)

Asked for by the operator on 2026-10-27. Built in one slice and approved after the merge ("committed work counts as ratified").

| Slice | Delivers | State |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| E1 | `$DELPHI_HOME/logs/delphi.log.jsonl`, `delphi logs`, `delphi debug bundle`, `delphi doctor` check 14, console error boundary | merged (#256) |
| E2 | `tool_failed` records why a call failed (`timeout`, `denied`, `invalid_args`, `tool_error`, from the first line of the result) and the names of its arguments, never a value or a hash of one; the same kind is stored as `failure` on `tool.result` and `harness.bridge_call` and counted per tool as `invalid_args` and `denied` by `observe::ToolCount` and `toolreport` (spec 033 §6). Amended 2026-11-07 at the operator's approval (Council action 2) | built 2026-11-07 (PR pending): `tools::failure_kind`, `tools::arg_keys`, `engine.rs`, `harness_run.rs`, `observe.rs`, `toolreport.rs`. Not built: `repeat_calls` (a hash of arguments can be guessed back; the loop nudge is the signal); a kind for a CLI seat's own native tools (`harness.tool` carries no outcome); a column for the kind in the console or `delphi tools`. Logs written before this carry no kind |

## Operator-authorized tests (spec 092, approved 2026-11-07)

**Approved by the operator on 2026-11-07** ("approve all of the above": the sandboxed-shell-only first slice and the recommended answers to OD-1 to OD-5). **S1 does not start until a red-team re-review of the text in a readable form has passed.** It narrowly amends spec 016's "Red is never allowed" for **headless runs only**: a test the operator authorized may run named Red actions, and an agent may *request* an authorization with its context and risk (the request is inert, staged at L0, and never effective for the run that wrote it). Council `t_0003` (stakes high) chose **approve with changes, narrower first**: a sandboxed shell command only, matched by exact canonical argv with a pinned binary, a mandatory OS sandbox, a disposable project snapshot and a required run binding. Its red-team seat's PATH-shadowing and worktree-poisoning P0s are closed in the text. 
| Slice | Content | State |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| S0 | this spec | approved 2026-11-07; the red-team re-review of v2 (readable) is still to run before S1. ## Benchmarking, context and memory (specs 048, 049, 050; drafted 2026-10-28)

Drafted from a review of LangChain / LangSmith benchmarking, memory and context practice (recollection, unverified). Delphi uses no LangChain code; these take methods. **049 and 050 were approved by the operator on 2026-10-28**, with their provisional decisions accepted and the source check waived (every technique ships `off` or proposes only, and 048 measures it). **048 was approved by the operator on 2026-11-05**, with its provisional decisions standing and the source check waived (OD-1, as for 049 and 050); BM6 stays gated on OD-3 (the operator picks the external subset; the GAIA-style anchor is still only under consideration). 049 refines slices H2/H3 of spec 045 and §4.3 of spec 030; 048 supplies the measuring instruments 045 H9 needs. A 049 or 050 slice that *measures* against 048 (CX2's retention numbers, CX4, CX5, CX6, ML6) waits for 048 BM1-BM3 to be built; ML1, ML2, ML3 and CX1's code can start now, with defaults `off`.

| Spec | Slice | Content | State |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| 048 | BM1 | harness arm kind (fixed model) and efficiency columns in the 037 report | built 2026-11-06: an `[[arm]]` with `base = "<arm>"` and `[arm.loop]` switches takes its base's template home, `expect_models` and overlay (`train_run.rs` `parse_arms`; switch names and value kinds checked by `loopctl::check_switch`, so a typo is refused before a run; applied to the run's copy of `config.toml`, `[loop]` not being a pinned block). Rows keep turns, tool calls by tool with errors, nudges, hard stops, budget warnings, verify turns, escalations and cache read share (`efficiency_of`, reusing `toolreport` and `ctxacct`). `delphi train report` prints an EFFORT table and a HARNESS EFFECT section per model: pass rate with the noise (one standard error of the difference), tokens, turns and calls per pass, and the OD-2 verdict (`costlier, not better` over 25 % tokens per pass with the rate inside the noise; `a trade` for more passes at that cost; `no evidence` under 3 seeds). Tests: AC-1 (arm and base on one model, case and seed, both in the report) and AC-2 (the flag). Not built: harness arms that vary the tool set or a prompt section (switches and overlay only); per-tool names in the report (rows keep `by_tool`); no live run (scripted fake binary only) |
| 048 | BM2 | handoff fidelity eval (paraphrase loss) | built 2026-11-06 (PR pending): `train_items.rs` (seeded generator of hash, path, number, code line and quoted-sentence items; byte-for-byte survival; shared with BM3) and `train_fidelity.rs` (`delphi train fidelity run` scores scripted verbatim, paraphrase and handle relays at the seat and at the operator, with a chart-ready table and `--json`; `gen` writes a case for a real model). New check kind `exact_items` (`path`, `min`) in `train.rs` / `train_run.rs`. AC-3 tested (paraphrase scores 0.0, verbatim 1.0; the handle relay is lossy at the seat and 1.0 at the operator, modelling CX-6). First live run 2026-11-07 (`fidelity/fid-7-10`, 10 items, `solo-haiku`, one seed, a Claude subscription seat; about 240K tokens counting cache reads, two runs): the first run scored 0 of 10 and was an instrument defect, not paraphrase loss (the prompt said "in the project", the model wrote `@project/artifact.md`, and both `read_file` and `list_dir` were refused because a project is addressed as `@project/<dir name>/`; fixed by giving the full path, `train_run::REPO_DIR`). The second run kept **10 of 10** items byte for byte. Haiku read the file itself and did not dispatch the investigator, so only the Orchestrator-to-operator hop was exercised. One seed, one small artifact: it shows the check works end to end and says nothing yet about how models differ. Not built: scoring the seat's own report from a real run (only the final answer is scored, as the deliverable); no handle relay in the engine (049 CX4); a larger artifact or a prompt that forces the seat hop, to stress a model |
| 048 | BM3 | context retention eval | built 2026-11-07 (PR pending, stacked on BM2): `train_retention.rs` (`delphi train retention run`): a seeded session with N exact facts told early and routine work after, five scripted conditions (no compaction, summary, summary with a handle to the dropped turns kept whole, the same with the handle ignored, a new session), a scripted model that answers only from what it sees and follows a handle only if it is the kind that does; chart-ready table and `--json`. Reuses BM2's `train_items.rs` and `exact_items`. AC-5 tested (summary 0%, handle followed 100%, ignored 0%; the handle costs under 60 characters). Not built: a live run (needs the compaction, 045 H3), multi-turn scenario cases (`train run` does not run scenarios yet), digest forms (049 CX5) |

Not built: images (the fetcher is text-only); archives beyond Gutenberg; the Gutendex answer shape is unverified against the live service |

| SK8a | spec 057: generated banner, bibliography table, mechanical citation check; replaces the 055 §6 per-sentence rule | built (`library_page_check.rs`, `library_pages.rs`, `delphi pages show\|check`): banner and bibliography table the daemon writes, `[s-…]`/`[c-…]` markers with one per paragraph, mechanical check (missing, retired, purged, disproved or private-on-external records, misstated status, verbatim hash-checked quotes, uncited tables, unknown images and links), unverified-figure flags in the sidecar; the SK5 per-sentence and content-word rule is gone. Source markers are `[s-<source id>]` (source ids are slugs, not `s-…`) |
| SK8b | spec 057: validator pass (contradiction and fabricated-citation checks), one rewrite, claims-only fallback | built (`library_page_validate.rs`, `claims add --origin`): `build_page_checked` runs the validator (V2b judge pattern, tool-less, role `validator`, budget reserved first) over the page and its material; the daemon classifies findings from its own records (a contradiction with a validated or cited record, or a fabricated citation, fails; a noted-record contradiction passes under Contested; unplaceable records fail closed); one rewrite with the findings, then the claims-only page with the reason in the banner; verdict in the sidecar; status stays `generated`; promote-to-claim makes a noted claim with `origin: page:<key>`. The CLI stays mechanical-only; engine and writer-span wiring is SK8c |
| SK8c | spec 057: search, gather, write, cache by source hash, `delphi pages search` | built (`library_page_search.rs`, `page_run.rs`, `pages.*` RPCs, `delphi pages search\|list\|pin\|unpin`, `[library.pages]`): gather by content words (claims ≤ 12, sources ≤ 6; private left out of external pages and queries, and a query naming a private source is not sent); the writer span on role `writer` writes the draft, `build_page_checked` checks it; cache `pages/<key>.md` + sidecar keyed by query, material hashes, writer identity, template version and scope, stale on changed or gone material, expired `external_ttl`, or a new matching record; pinning, LRU eviction under one twentieth of the library budget, history kept first; a double failure cached 1 hour; `per_day` and the validator share (040 §20, journal) gate a new page, never a cached one; external search through the ratified `[search]` engines, captures noted, not validated |
| SK8d | spec 057: console page view, keys, Pages in the library, digest line | built (`ui/src/pages.ts`, `ui/test/pages.test.ts`, `digest_review.rs`): the Pages view after Claims in the library cycle (`ctrl+l`): the question, the records found at once (`pages.results`), the page beside them (`pages.search`) with the banner, status glyphs and the bibliography table as the daemon wrote them; saved pages listed as pages; keys `s` pin, `d` dispute a statement's cited claim (SK5 action), `p` promote to a noted claim, `o` open a cited source in Sources; a saved page whose material changed is one digest card (keep, dismiss); the wait spinner takes `stillMotion` from `motion.ts` |
| SK8e | spec 057 §5.1 (057-A1, council 012): gather counters, parity fixtures, baseline measurement | built 2026-10-30 (`GatherStats` on `Gathered`, also in the `pages.results` reply as `gather`; `stored_text_measured`; parity fixtures in `library_tests.rs`; ignored `gather_baseline_report`). Baseline, release build, synthetic library with 2% large documents: 100 sources 15 ms; 1,000 sources 151 ms (meta list 25%, read 13%, hash 9%, tokenize 30%); 3,000 sources 479 ms (same shape). Read, hash and tokenize are about 51% of gather, under the 70% bar |
| SK8f | spec 057 §5.1: word-set cache behind `[library.pages] word_cache`, only if SK8e shows read, hash and tokenize at 70% or more of gather time | not warranted by the SK8e baseline (51% at 1,000 sources); closed unbuilt 2026-10-30. Reopen if a real library measures at 70% or more, or if gather exceeds the latency the console needs. Tokenizing and meta parsing, not file reads, are now the larger share |

Amended with 055 (2026-10-29, additive): 012 §11 (`fact` kind, provisional), 022 §5, 023 §2.1 and step 7a, 024 §4.1a, 027 §2.3a, `schemas/bundle.schema.json` (`sections.sources`).

## Discovered model assignment (spec 056, approved 2026-10-29)

Asked for by the operator on 2026-10-28: no hard-coded favorites; discover what is available and assign the best model per agent and task; more effort for planning and specs, the lowest acceptable model for following a build plan; promote and nerf an agent; the knowledge in the core knowledge base. Approved 2026-10-29 ("ratify and build it", all §7 recommendations accepted; OD-1 amends D-15 in spec 035, recorded as D-15a; OD-5: the default stays `static` until a 048 benchmark). Revised 2026-10-29 on operator direction : role floor plus preferred class instead of a role default (OD-1), no provider default, ratify the pool once, quota as a live input with per-role reserve, council diversity, a provider array, and trial of unclassified models (OD-4, OD-6); single-provider operators not penalized, single-model low-diversity councils (OD-7); local `emergency` stop-gap that saves state (OD-8, M6).

| M4b | ratchet and suggestions from retrospectives (the original M4) | not started |

| M6 | `emergency` local class: save state when every seat is exhausted, re-verify on recovery, doctor line | built and wired (pieces in `delphi-engine/src/emergency.rs` (#496), called by the engine through `emergency_run.rs`; `[routing] emergency_endpoint`/`emergency_model`, off by default, local endpoints only; `Class::Emergency` below every floor and refused by `route::ineligible` under both assignments; trigger only when every ratified seat is out; `Task` allow-list read/summarise/write-state; `save_state` writes a labeled `emergency` record under `<mission>/handoffs/`; `recover` re-checks branches, uncommitted files, journal pointers and PRs and removes the label only after a floor-meeting verifier answers; doctor and `capabilities --models` print the fixed line). Wired in the follow-up slice: `model_turn` calls `Engine::emergency_step` after each route. On `Route::NoModel` with `trigger` true it gathers git and mission-log facts by code, the emergency model (a tool-less `one_shot_raw` call through the ordinary model client, `permits` checked when `ModelWriter` is built) writes the prose, `save_state` writes the labeled record under `<mission>/handoffs/`, and `emergency.saved` is logged; the task then waits for a real seat as before. While a labeled record stands no span takes a turn and no harness fallback runs; the first span routed to a seat that meets its role's floor makes that seat the verifier (`recover`: label removed only on a non-empty answer; the emergency model and below-floor models are refused) and `emergency.recovered` is logged; a failed, empty or below-floor verification logs `emergency.blocked` and stops the span with the label on. The events show in /observe and the chat log. With the keys unset `emergency_step` returns first and nothing changes (test). Tested with a scripted model (whole path, failed and empty verifier, a pending record blocks a new dispatch, unset = no change) and once live on Ollama (gemma4 wrote the record; PR body has the outcome). Not proven: open PRs are not read (the engine reaches no network; the recorded list is carried over); no real paid-seat quota outage has run it (the live check used local models as the ratified seat); the verifier is the span's own seat, so a pool of only local models cannot verify for a `steady`-floor role and that mission stays held |

## Project scopes, scaffolding, indexes and promotion (spec 058, approved 2026-10-30)

Approved and ratified by the operator on 2026-10-30. Delphi always runs; registered directory scopes add project-specific context and memory to the shared home layer, including from subdirectories. Unregistered directories use a persistent default-project layer plus shared home memory. Git is optional. Project roots are centrally registered, and explicit registration/init scaffolds project files and the generated `_index.md`. `/promote` aliases `/retro` for reviewed project-to-home memory promotion, optionally carrying references to eligible trusted sources.

| :-- | :-- | :-- |

| PS2 | Home + project memory/context routing, provenance labels, project isolation | built in part 2026-10-30 (`delphi-engine/src/scope.rs`: resolution with fail-closed registry, home+project `layered_sources` with layer labels and labelled overrides, `route_staged`; `learn::project_staged` writes a mission's staged notes to its project layer from a `<mission>/scope` file and to no layer when the id is unregistered or spoofed). Built 2026-10-30 (PS2b): `mission.open` carries the client `cwd` and a newly created mission is stamped with the resolved project (`EngineService::mission_at`; no cwd, unresolvable or ambiguous/corrupt registry stamps nothing = home-only), and `scope::prompt_layer` puts the mission's own project layer (own-role memory, knowledge, context; never staged, never another project's) into the span prompt as a labelled `@runtime/project-layer` slice, span tainted to L0. Deferred: moving unscoped missions to the default project, loading the project layer into the `Kb` index (search/`kb::sources` still home-only; project items stay out of search until then), operator-chosen destination in a proposal, project-layer `heuristics`/`lessons` ratification |
| PS3 | Explicit no-Git scaffold, project marker, collision handling and migration; amend 006 clean-repo rule | built in part 2026-10-30 (#381: `project init`, marker, create-only `_index.md` stub; PS4 now records its hash at init; migration not done) |

## Encyclopedia search pages, inline media, and reputable expansion (spec 097, approved 2026-10-31)

Approved by the operator on 2026-10-31. Makes the Wikipedia-style Encyclopedia Summary Page the primary knowledge base search result, rendered through `ui/src/markdown.ts` and `ui/src/image.ts`, authored by the `writer` role under `skills/encyclopedia-synthesis/SKILL.md`, cached with YAML frontmatter `valid_until` (`[1, 365]` days at the `writer`'s discretion), synthesized immediately on search from local sources, and expandable (`e`) with reputable external research and licensed images.

## Agent episodic memory, trace distillation, and tool-conditioned retrieval (spec 065, approved 2026-11-01)

Approved by the operator on 2026-11-01 after a four-seat adversarial council review (architect, red-team, strategist, orchestrator). Establishes zero-token deterministic trace harvesting from execution event streams (`events.jsonl`), immutable daemon-enforced frontmatter and taint stamping (`min(span.taint)`), quota bounding (max 1 per task, max 3 per mission), and tool-conditioned hybrid archival retrieval (`zone: episodes`) while preserving the 100% frozen boot KV cache prefix.

| Slice | Content | State |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| E1 | `schemas/episode.schema.json`, `trace_miner.rs` event harvester, daemon taint stamping, deduplication, and schema verification in `docs.rs` | built, corrected 2026-11-13: the schema, the harvester and its tests were built, but nothing in the daemon called `harvest_episodes`, so no episode file was ever written. Spec 108 M4 (pull request open) wires it: every task end (done, escalated, failed, cancelled) writes `missions/<id>/traces/<role>-<task>.md` off the task's path, at most one per task and three per mission, deduplicated against the mission's episodes, through the secret filter. Its stamping was also wrong and is fixed: the tier is the lowest `span.taint` of the task's spans capped at `internal_generated`, and `external_untrusted` with no taint event (it was `internal_empirical`); a missing time no longer becomes a fixed date |
| E2 | KB indexing (`zone: episodes`), tool/skill/error tokenization in `kb_fts_ident`, and role isolation in `kb.rs` | built; until spec 108 M4 it indexed only files a test wrote, since none were written. The vector scope now reads a hyphenated role (`red-team-t_0001.md`) whole |
| E3 | Agent tool `recall_memory` and engine wiring to unified retrieval service (`specs/064`) | built; it had no episodes to return before spec 108 M4 |
| E4 | Proactive retry-ladder bridge hint injection and `/retro` candidate supersession | built; as E3, inert until episodes were written (spec 108 M4) |

## Spec 098: where agents run (2026-11-10, approved)

A draft for the operator: host first, the inside exceptions (Council, panel and red-team seats of other families), a `locus` declaration, `route` for any query, generated task-scoped agents with guards, the Builder in the host against an approved spec, and a research path that works the first time. Written after Council `t_0001` (2026-11-10) reviewed the outline, which escalated; the draft answers its findings in §15 and Council `t_0002` reviewed the draft (PROCEED-WITH-CHANGES, 9 P0s, two seats could not read the text); its findings are answered in §15.1 and the slices now run in waves behind flags (§13.1). Councils `t_0004` and `t_0005` then read the full text and both ruled PROCEED-WITH-CHANGES (answered in §15.2; red-team's Round 2 unreadable in both, so a rerun is owed). The research redesign keeps everything found and adds a `corroborated` status with a `trusted_sources` flag (§7.5, §7.6); §19 adds progress visibility. Council `t_0006` (on a substantiated/vetted ladder) advised shipping only `corroborated` plus `trusted_sources`; the higher tiers go to their own spec 100, built before release, with 040 §15.4's audit as a roll-back trigger, not a promotion gate (OD-11, decided 2026-11-10); `delphi check spec` now passes. One spec by the operator's choice. Research is limited while the knowledge base and the trusted list are small; outside search fills the gap and grows Delphi (§7.5). Approved by the operator on 2026-11-10 ("approve spec 098"); OD-1 to OD-6 and OD-8 to OD-11 take their recommendations, and OD-7 (inside fetch) was decided the same day: "7a yes, 7b off", so S1d builds 7a only and 7b is not built. The review with a completed red-team Round 2 was not run before approval.

**Roll-back by feature (spec 098 §13.2).** The unit is a slice's merge commit on `main`; `git revert -m 1 <commit>` undoes it. Revert newest first, and **revert S11's own merge commit (recorded in the last row when it merges) before any feature it pulled a flag from**, because S11 deleted the code that gated them. A revert deletes no data. `delphi update --rollback` or `--version vX.Y.Z --allow-downgrade` is the release-level way back. No downgrade drill has been run for any row (S12).

| S6 standalone red team, `ask_family` | `8fe80c6b` (#627) | `git revert -m 1 8fe80c6b` (after #648) | not run |

**Known gaps found by a live check, 2026-11-13** (the operator asked "check the search_kb issue" after the spec 103 M4 live check could not exercise `host.search_kb`):

- **Fixed:** a host's first `search_kb` on a workspace whose index was never built was answered `knowledge base: no such table: kb_meta` (a daemon error; `local/state.db` also holds state that is not the index, so the file exists before the first index). It now answers empty with a hint to run `delphi kb index`. Present on 0.6.0 and main. Test: `service.rs::a_host_search_before_the_index_was_ever_built_answers_empty_with_a_hint_not_an_error`.
- **Fixed (operator: "follow your recommendation for defect 2", 2026-11-13): §7.2 "a noted entry is searchable at once" did not hold for a project-scoped host mission.** `host.propose_kb_entry` with `claims` stored the entry and answered `"searchable": true`, but the daemon stamps a host mission with a project scope (`<mission>/scope`), `learn::project_staged` then wrote the note under that project's layer (`local/projects/<id>/staged/`), and the index (`kb::sources`) reads only `local/staged/` while the host's project search reads only the `context` and `knowledge` zones, so the entry was found by neither path, even after a refresh. Now: the note is projected when it is stored (`service.rs host_noted`), and the host's `search_kb` reads the staged notes of the ONE scope the daemon stamped on that host's own mission, from the files (`host_noted::host_scope`, `mcp_project::scoped_staged_files` and `rank_noted_files`, filtered to the daemon's own `staged/host-<session>/n<k>.md` paths by `private_read::noted_entry`), fenced and labelled `noted`, never another project's layer and never added to the shared index; a host whose mission has no scope is served by the index as before. Tests: `a_noted_entry_is_found_by_the_same_hosts_search_right_after_it_is_stored`, `a_host_in_one_project_never_finds_another_projects_noted_entries`, `a_private_noted_entry_returned_to_its_own_scope_marks_the_host_and_the_answer_is_unchanged_by_it`, `a_host_with_no_scope_still_searches_the_shared_index_as_before_and_is_not_shown_twice`, `a_scoped_noted_entry_is_never_added_to_the_shared_index`. The 0.6.0 build the operator runs has no noted-entry form at all (`claims` is refused with "`title` is required"), so nothing running is affected until a build with this is installed. Still true: hosts in the same project share that project's noted entries (as home-only hosts shared `local/staged/` before projects existed).
- **Not reproduced: the "hang".** `kb.search` and the console-only `kb.index` (called under a pty) returned in 0.07 to 1.8 s on a fresh scratch home with this build and with the installed 0.6.0 (which embeds a model), and the daemon answered a ping straight after; at rest it used under 1% CPU.

## Spec 100: the confidence ladder (2026-11-10, approved)

Approved by the operator on 2026-11-10 ("approve spec 099 and spec 100"). It adds the tiers above `corroborated` (spec 098): `substantiated` (3+ origins, 1+ trusted source) and `vetted` (5+ origins, 2+ trusted sources and the validator's own checks pass, which is 040's advisory `would_ratify` made visible), a fast lane that changes queue order only, an accept-all-vetted workflow, and KB-prior tags. **040 §15.4's audit is a roll-back trigger, not a promotion gate** (operator decision, 2026-11-10; Council `t_0006` advised the opposite and is declined at the operator's direction). The operator runs the audit by hand once there is data; a tier that fails is latched off and the labels fall back. The tier numbers are defaults to calibrate, not pre-registered. It depends on spec 098 (approved, merged in #612). Hours are estimates.

| Slice | Content | Hours | State |
| :-- | :-- | --: | :-- |

| L1 | the tier table in `delphi-core`, labels, glyphs, docs, the `[research] tiers` setting | 10-16 | built (draft PR): tier table, labels, `[research.tiers]`, grants-nothing test; the `would_ratify` writer is L2 |
| L2 | the fast lane (queue order only) and `would_ratify` shown as `vetted` | 12-18 | built (draft PR): `Library::record_would_ratify` (called only from the validator's loop in `fact_serve`, after an advisory verdict; stores the counts it was made against, so a change of sources drops it); `validation_candidates` + `lane_order` (the lane: a higher rung first; `[research.tiers] fast_lane_enabled`); `delphi validation queue` lists the library's candidates in lane order (ids, rungs, counts). **Background worker built (draft PR, `[validator] background`, default OFF):** `fact_background.rs` picks the next library claim in the lane's order and the daemon's timer runs it through the validator's own loop with `background` set by the daemon (a span's request has the flag cleared), capped at advisory, charged to the 040 §20.2 background share (10% of the install's trailing week) and a per-day cap of 100,000 tokens (hypothesis); it waits for 5 minutes of operator quiet, no running task or validator job, at most 10 audit cards, and an unrolled vetted tier; skips protected-class, private-source, contested and twice-tried claims; status in `delphi validation queue` and `delphi doctor`; tested with a fake clock and fixtures, never run against a live validator. Open: the idle window, tick interval and day cap are hypotheses to tune from the status numbers. The `protected` tag is not derived on library claims (the validator's own protected-class check keeps such a claim from a would_ratify). Never run against a live validator. |
| L6 | the roll-back: `tier-audit`, `tier-report`, `tier rollback` and `restore`, the tier latch and the event (built before L3) | 12-18 | built (draft PR, stacked on L2): `delphi validation tier-audit` (random draw stratified by source class, claim first, answer recorded, then the sources), `tier-report` (audited count, errors, rule-of-three or Wilson 95% bound, not-yet-audited, last-audit age, floor of 30, **no verdict below the floor**, roll-backs), `tier threshold` (the operator's limit, no default), `tier rollback`/`restore` (reason required; restore starts a new audit batch); the latch is `tier_latch.json` in the validator's state directory, re-read about once a second by the daemon (`set_tier_latch_file` at boot), so it survives a restart and a roll-back from the CLI reaches a running daemon; a held vetted reads substantiated, a held substantiated reads corroborated (and takes vetted with it); `tier_rollback` event in the audit journal; `delphi needs` names a rolled-back tier; all `tier-*` refuse inside an agent seat. **Not built:** `batch-accepted` items and their `re-audit due` flag and the accept-all disable (no accept-all until L3; `reaudit_due` is recorded as 0), the calibration report of §8 (L4, since built), the console card for a tier roll-back. **Follow-up built (PR open):** the other two hard failures are recorded on the claim and trip the tier at once: the `protected` tag is derived from the claim's text by the validator's own check (such a claim never rises), and `refresh_support` records `protected_class_reached_vetted` or `poisoned_page_passed` (a held page that addresses its reader, the validator's own `addresses_reader`) for a claim that reached vetted or was batch-accepted; `accept_all::latch_on_hard_failures` trips the vetted tier through the audit's roll-back and its `tier_rollback` event (trigger `hard_failure`), from the validator's loop and the operator's accept, and a failure before the last restore does not trip it again. Never run against a real audit. |

NOT built: any live run, and the Ink console has not been looked at with the card |
| L4 | the per-tier calibration report | 10-16 | built (draft PR): `delphi validation tier-report` now ends with a calibration section per rung (corroborated, substantiated, vetted): claims that read the rung, later contested, later disproved; at or above the floor of 30 the observed share and its upper bound, below it the numbers and no verdict; a new top-level `reached` mark on a claim (first time each rung was read, written by the daemon in `refresh_support`, never removed) makes the join "by the rung at the time", and `Library::tier_outcomes` counts it. Counts start when the mark does, so early numbers are a lower bound. Not run against real data |
| L5 | KB-prior tags: support through origins, cycle check, contradiction block, track record from operator-accepted history | 16-24 | built (draft PR, stacked on L4): support through origins and the cycle check were already how the count works (a generated page is zeroed as `derived`), now proven by a fixture at library level (a page built from a substantiated claim adds no origin to a claim that cites it); `Library::contradicts_validated` marks the contradicting claim contested (daemon only; never writes the validated fact; refuses a non-validated target, itself, or a claim that is itself validated) and a fixture shows a 3-origin claim cannot rise past it; the track record is split in the `track` tag and on `Reliability` into `accepted` (operator, one at a time), `batch_accepted` and `auto_validated`, and `standing()` returns only `accepted`. NOT built: any reader of `standing()` (nothing may become a trusted source from history; the list stays the operator's). **Contradiction search extended (2026-11-12, the operator's "extend the search", draft PR):** the validator's contradiction step now also reads the knowledge base's VALIDATED facts, locally and before any web search (so it runs with no search engine and adds no outbound request): `ValidatedFacts` (trait in `fact_run.rs`, implemented by the library: validated claims ranked by shared content words, at most `VALIDATED_FACTS_JUDGED` = 3, never the claim's own text, never an unvalidated claim) offers each to the SAME entailment judge that reads a web passage; a `Contradicts` verdict ends the check as `Conflicting` (never ratified) and puts only the validated fact's id on the report (`contradicted_validated`, also in the `validator.verdict` event); the validator's loop (`fact_serve.rs`) hands the ids to `Library::record_contradictions`, which calls `contradicts_validated` for the library claims with that text, so a 3-origin claim reads contested and the validated fact is byte-identical afterwards. The word-overlap thresholds (3 shared content words and 40 percent of the shorter text) are hypotheses. Net test extended: only the library and the validator's loop may name the recorder, and only `fact_run.rs`, the library and `fact_serve.rs` may name the trait. NOT built: a judge for a contradiction the word overlap does not retrieve (a paraphrase that shares fewer than three content words is not offered), and nothing has run against real data |

## Spec 101: library practice for research (2026-11-11, approved)

Approved by the operator on 2026-11-11 ("spec 101 is approved"). Origin: A Scout reviewed library science for gaps in the research workstream; Council `t_0007` (architect, red-team, strategist) advised which to include. The operator ruled API-based citation chaining in scope and that an archived copy is captured as a labelled, searchable page (2026-11-11). Slices are buildable once this pull request merges. No outside claim in the spec has been checked against its source.

Not built or unverified: nothing has run against a live search provider or host; the shared egress check (#626's `egress::check`) is not on main, so the log uses the library's own outbound check (`library::may_leave`); the corroboration pass still treats an unknown private flag as public (098 S1a) **Flag `[research] search_log` pulled in S11 (spec 098 §13.2): the feature is always on.** |

Verified from Crossref's documentation page and the repository README: CSV, the column names, the four `RetractionNature` values, the daily update. NOT built: a trigger from a lost quote (the older version in `versions/` already holds it), a trigger inside the daemon's host-claim refetch (a claim is public only after a daemon refetch finds its quote, so a never-fetched URL sends nothing), the search log line for an archive lookup (slice A's log is per research run and this runs outside one), `archive: unavailable` in the coverage report. The `id_` raw-copy form works but is not in the archive's own documentation (NOT VALIDATED); rate limits, terms and authentication are not stated on the API's page. Nothing ran against the live archive in a test (a loopback fixture stands in). Decided 2026-11-11: archive lookups stay operator-triggered and are not journalled (§5 item 9). |

The origin count gains `Cite::work` (the source's normalised DOI from `retraction::doi_of`): two citations of the same work, however spelled or hosted, are ONE origin (`corroboration::support`), so it can only lower a count; named tests at the pure function and through `cites_of`. A net test pins that only the matching files name an identifier and that nothing calls `orcid` or `ror` yet (the library records no author or publisher identifier, so they have no data to match). Verified: ROR's identifier page (format, regex, example `02mhbdp94`) and the generator code it links (the checksum), by hand against the example; ORCID's FAQ (last character is a checksum, 10 written `X`, example `…233X`). NOT VALIDATED: ORCID's support page (the ISO 7064 algorithm and that `X` must be a capital) answered 403 to a fetch; the code is checked against two real iDs (the FAQ's and `0000-0002-1825-0097`) and accepts a lowercase `x`, which only widens matching |


## Spec 102: the exchange record (2026-11-11, approved)

Approved by the operator on 2026-11-11 ("approve spec 102"), after Council `t_0009` (approve with named changes) and OD-8 decided as "hold" (the opt-out default stays off until the P0s are closed; one release). Drafted for a Council review. The operator asked for a project history with the operator's own words and deeper provenance; Council `t_0008` advised against storing transcripts, and the operator then chose a smaller unit, the exchange record (a prompt, a capped reply summary, an outcome and references). Decided by the operator on 2026-11-11: summaries are written after a merge, a research run, a panel, a Council and whenever a work artifact is created in the project folder; the store is opt-out, with a question at install (the Council advised opt-in; this departs from it). The slices start only after the verification gates of specs 098, 100 and 101 pass.

| Slice | Content | Hours | State |
| :-- | :-- | --: | :-- |
| S0 | this spec, its review and amendments (062 §8.1, 084 §3, 049 CX-5, 055 §10.1) | 6-10 | approved 2026-11-11 (pull request open) |
| S1 | the pointer record, hash check, `unresolvable`, session-id search for Claude | 8-12 (spec §8 now says 9-13) | built as a library, no surface yet (PR open; the operator asked for S1 and S2 on 2026-11-12, ahead of the gates, because neither touches an egress path): `history_pointer.rs` has the pointer `{tool, session_id, range, hmac, kind}` (kinds `operator-words`, `agent-words`, `unread`); the keyed hash (HMAC-SHA256 under a 32-byte local key, `local/history.key`, owner-only, made on first need, a readable or garbled key refused); `match_reported` (a host-reported text that is a user-typed or assistant line of a transcript the 062 reader recognises gives a pointer, anything else is `Reported`); `resolve` (the 062 reader, then the role check, then the keyed hash; `Unresolvable` with a fixed reason, never validated); `find_sessions` (the session id searched under the projects root only: direct sub-folders, no link followed, no id that could name a path, every copy tried and the hash decides). Not built: nothing calls it (the exchange record that stores a pointer is S3; the `kind: observed` label is S3's; no command, no config key); Claude Code is the only reader; no live run against a real `~/.claude/projects`. |
| S2 | the daemon timeline and report (events only, 4 KB cap, supersession, footer) | 10-14 (spec §8 now says 11-15) | built as a library, no surface yet (PR open, same pull request as S1): `history_report.rs` folds a mission's events into counts and ids (asked = operator `task.created`, done and failed tasks, provisional decisions made, raised and awaiting decisions, learn proposals and approvals as counts, files touched as a distinct count), shows a `decision.reverted` decision as superseded with the next decision of the same task as its replacement (never deleted), prints only fixed templates and ids that pass `safe_id` (so a hostile event string leaves no trace; plain ASCII), always carries the fixed `N events not counted` line (N from `read_events`, a tolerant reader that counts lines that do not parse, another schema major and a torn final line, plus folded events with no usable id), ends with the fixed footer, and `agent_view` fences it as data and shrinks the ids per line until it is at most 4,096 bytes (counts are never cut). Net test: nothing outside the two modules names them; they import only inert crates and name no socket, process or model call. Not built: nothing loads the view at session start (never always-loaded; a later slice), no command, no operator's long view (S5), no record text in it (S3 and later), no chain verification (S3). The replacement link is a reading, not a field: `decision.reverted` carries no replacement id, so the next `decision.provisional` of the same task stands in. |
| S3 | exchange-record capture, redaction, `private`, trigger wiring, pasted-block hashing | 18-26 (spec §8 now says 28-40) | built, dormant (PR open; the operator asked for it on 2026-11-12, ahead of the gates; `[history] enabled` stays `false`, so nothing is recorded, no key is made and no prompt is held until S8): `history_redact.rs` (the paste rule of §3.4: a span over `paste_chars`, a 40-character run at 4.5 bits or more, or code-like text is stored as a keyed hash and a length unless the daemon saw it typed, and a mark only adds; the core redactor plus the ten shapes of the corpus; control characters stripped; the 2,000-character cut with the keyed hash of the full text; the 200-character reply fields) and `history_record.rs` (the record with its fixed fields; `kind` computed by the daemon, `resolved` through the S1 check; append-only and chained by HMAC under the local key, verified on every read with `chain broken` for the break and everything after it and nothing repaired; `[history]` settings with the safe defaults; the budget stop; the per-project `history.off` honoured; `Channel::History` registered with the one egress check, `release` always refused and `for_prompt` deny by default, allow only for the host's own family; `parse_host_exchange` refusing any field the daemon computes and a second outcome). Triggers wired to the events that exist: research run = `research.finished`, panel = `council.verdict` with `venue: panel`, Council = `council.verdict` otherwise, work artifact = the artifact prompt log's append in `log_artifact_prompts` (the record references the 070 entries and holds no prompt text). **Follow-ups (draft PR, one PR after S3 and S6; the operator asked for them on 2026-11-12):** (1) the **merge** trigger is now wired: the daemon reads the merge ledger itself on a 5-second timer, only while `[history] enabled`, from the ledger's end at the time it began watching, complete lines only and at most 256 KB a call, reduced to the pull request number (`history_merge.rs`), and hands the row to the default mission's tap; `delphi merge` still has no daemon connection, names no history module (a net test) and cannot write the store. (2) The host's **reply summary**: `record_session_summary` takes the optional `prompt`, `session_id`, `asked`, `did`, `open` (each reply field at most 200 characters, scrubbed and redacted by the S3 pipeline, labelled self-reported). A host cannot create a record, so the daemon remembers (in memory, at most 8, for at most an hour) a trigger it saw while holding no prompt of its own, and the host's report **completes the latest one**, with `kind` `resolved` if the host's session id finds the words as a user message in its transcript, else `reported`; the outcome and the observed facts stay the daemon's and no outcome field is accepted; with history off the fields are ignored and the answer says `off`. A report with no trigger, or no prompt, writes nothing. **Not built:** (3) the writer of `cites` (the spec does not say who writes it or from what span; see S6), (4) typing-rate evidence (the spec gives no protocol for how the console proves a span was typed at typing rate, per span, to a daemon that must not trust a host's mark; every prompt stays `NotSeen`, so the length rule applies), a reply for a prompt the daemon itself observed (the record is final when written and a late reply cannot amend an append-only chain), and a merge run by a host agent (the ledger names no mission, so only the default mission's prompt is concluded). Not built: the commands, purge, the install question (S4), the console view (S5, its own PR), the audit (S7); no live run. |
| S4 | the install question, settings, `history off|purge|delete|status`, doctor, first-run notice | 10-14 (spec §8 now says 14-20) | built, off by default (PR open; the operator asked for it on 2026-11-12, after the S3 and S6 follow-ups): `history_manage.rs` (`off` writes a marker file the daemon checks at every append, so it takes effect at once and deletes nothing, globally at `local/history.off` or per project; `purge` (all, or `--since` a duration, a date or a time) and `delete <id>` replace each erased record with a tombstone that keeps its id, its time, the hash of the record before it, its own keyed hash and a keyed seal and no text, so the line count and ids are unchanged and `history_record::read` verifies the chain through every tombstone; a record that had already failed the chain check leaves a tombstone that is itself a break, and a line that cannot be read is voided, so erasing never launders a tampered record; the rewrite is atomic, owner-only and taken under the same cross-process lock the daemon appends under; `status` and the `doctor` line print fixed templates, counts, sizes, the chain state and the time of the last record, never record text), `delphi history off|purge|delete|status` (the operator's, refused inside an agent seat, no daemon method, and `purge`/`delete` ask for `yes` or take `--yes`), two new settings `[history] retention_days` (90, a hypothesis; deletes nothing, status counts the records past it) and `timeline` (true; `history_report::timeline_of_if` honours it), and `history_consent.rs` (the install question and first-run notice written word for word from §7, **shown since S8**: `SHOW_AT_INSTALL` is `false` until S8; only a `Human` made by `Human::at_terminal`, which refuses an agent seat, the host socket and a missing terminal, can record an answer; while the switch is on and nobody answered, `append` writes nothing); `delphi init` calls it and prints nothing today. **Not built:** the question at `delphi connect` (it has no workspace to keep the answer in), the first-run notice in the console (S5/S8), and any scrub of the daemon's in-memory prompt on `purge` (the daemon holds the last operator prompt in memory until its trigger fires or it stops; `off` keeps it from being written). Tested: the engine's history unit tests (91 pass; those for the manage and consent modules are new), 7 CLI tests (including a scan of the whole home for leftover text after a purge), the net test (the controls are named only by the files that wire them and the answer only by the consent module and the command). Nothing run against a live daemon. |
| S5 | the console view and `history_search` | 8-12 (spec §8 now says 4-6 for the view alone; `history_search` is S5b, deferred) | built, the operator's read-only view only (PR open; the operator asked for it on 2026-11-12, ahead of the gates): `/history [records]` in the Ink console calls the one new daemon method `history.view`, which is for the console scope alone (not on the narrator, relay or host lists, and refused to them by a test) and calls no model. `history_view.rs` reads the store with the S3 reader (the chain is checked end to end; nothing is repaired, no key or file is made) and the mission log with the S2 timeline, and returns the newest 50 records with: the check shown in words (`verified`, `chain broken` for that record and every later one, `unverifiable` when the key is missing), a pasted block as the first 12 characters of its keyed hash and its length (the text is not in the store), the host's account labelled `self-reported, not checked`, and counts only of verified, broken and hidden records (the outcome is drawn as a word and never counted). Every string is stripped of control characters (C0, C1, DEL, bidi overrides) and cut to its cap again at read time, and the console strips again before it draws. With history off it says so and names `[history] enabled in config.toml`, and writes nothing. Tests: 10 in `history_view.rs`, 1 in `service.rs` (scope and no model call), 11 in `ui/test/history.test.ts`, a net test (`the_operators_history_view_only_reads_draws_and_counts_nothing_that_grants`). Not built: `history_search` for agents (S5b, deferred by OD-5); the Rust consoles do not have the command (`rust: false`); no golden screen, because the output is a note, like `/context`'s. Run against no real store |
| S6 | the citation check: a decision cites a resolving, hash-matching span, else unsourced | 6-10 | built (draft PR, stacked on S3; the operator asked for it on 2026-11-12): `history_citation.rs`, a check derived at read time and stored nowhere. A decision reads `cited` only if its `decision.provisional` event carries a `cites` list of 1 to 8 pointers and every one resolves through the S1 check (transcript found under the Claude projects root, line of the claimed role and not a tool result, keyed hash matches); anything else reads `unsourced` with a fixed reason (no citation, malformed, too many, or the S1 reason). Shown in `decisions.list` (a `citation` field, printed by `/decisions show` as `source: cited …` or `source: unsourced (…)`) and as `N cited, M unsourced` in the S2 report (a timeline never given the check reads every decision unsourced). A citation changes no status, tier, trust or approval, and a cited decision ratifies exactly like an unsourced one. **Gap:** nothing writes `cites` yet (the decision event is built by the daemon from a tool call and has no transcript span to point at), so every decision reads `unsourced (no citation)` today, as it does with history off; the writer is a later slice (the S3 follow-ups left it out on purpose: spec 102 §6 says a summary's decisions must cite a pointer but not who writes `cites` on a `decision.provisional` event or from what span, and a model-supplied pointer proves nothing until it resolves, so the question goes to the operator). Not run live against a real transcript. |
| S7 | a red-team audit of redaction, purge and egress coverage | 10-14 (spec §8 now says 16-24) | audited, draft PR (the operator asked for it on 2026-11-12), dormant: nothing here turns anything on. **Redaction:** a corpus of 74 fake shapes designed from public rule lists before the redactor was read (`crates/delphi-engine/tests/history_redaction_corpus.rs`): 43 were caught, 31 were missed or only partly caught, 27 of those are fixed (vendor token prefixes, PGP blocks, `Authorization: Basic`, cookies, JSON and markdown keys, `redis://:pw@`, mysql `-p`, curl `-u`, netrc, 2FA seeds, labelled recovery phrases, zero-width and bidi splits, look-alike letters, full-width characters, HTML entities, `NAME=value\` continuations); the four left (one partly caught) are listed in the test with the reason (a secret re-spelled with spaces, an unlabelled quoted string, twelve unlabelled plain words, a token continued after a human label on a later line); 20 ordinary prompts (including Russian, Chinese and emoji) stay unchanged, and two known over-redactions (a commit hash, `password_hint: string`) are recorded. **Purge:** one real defect found and fixed: the running daemon kept the operator's last prompt in memory after `purge` and the next trigger wrote it back into the emptied store; `purge` now writes a time-only marker (`local/history.purged`) and the tap drops a held prompt noted before it. The zero-residue scan (engine test over the real task, tap, ledger and host-exchange paths, with a history-off baseline, hex, base64 and SHA-256 forms; CLI test over the debug bundle, `doctor`, `status`, `logs` and the whole home) finds none else. Stated limit: the same prompt is the task's own input and stays in the mission's event log and session log whether history is on or off, and the 070 artifact log is untouched by design; filesystem free blocks are not wiped. **Paste rule:** 32 typed and 32 pasted fixtures: false-paste 5 of 32 (16%, unchanged: long typed paragraphs and operator-written key-value or code lines); false-typed 11 of 32 (34%) before, 4 of 32 (12%) after adding log, diff, table, csv and SQL line shapes, which only hash more; what remains is short pasted text, which no length or shape rule can tell from typing (only input timing can, and none reaches the daemon yet). **Egress:** every history module is classified and names no network or process API; the History channel is registered; only a listed set of files names a history module and no relay, mail, connector, outbound, model, debug-bundle, export or update file does; `for_prompt` has no caller and defaults to deny; a later task's model request holds no record text. Relay, Connector, Mail and Outbound remain `Unaudited` (spec 103); this slice proves only that none of them reads the store. **Verdict:** S7 passes for the code it audited, with the residual risks for the operator to weigh before S8: redaction is best effort (the four listed gaps and anything unlisted), the paste rule keeps short pasted text, the mission's own logs keep the prompt, the four channels are unaudited, and nothing has run live. S8 is not marked ready. |
| S8 | turn the default on; show the install question and the first-run notice | 2-4 | built, draft PR (the operator's go, 2026-11-12: "go on S8"; S7 passed in #676 and spec 103 M1 to M4 wired the four channels). `[history] enabled` now defaults to `true`, **but nothing is recorded, held in memory or keyed until a person at a terminal has answered the install question** (`local/history.consent`; `Tap::recording` gates all five tap sites, so a prompt typed before the answer is not written after it). The question prints what is kept, where, how to stop it and what redaction cannot promise, and is asked by `delphi init` (before setup takes over) and by the first `delphi` start in a terminal (which is how an install that existed before is asked, once); an agent seat, a script, `--non-interactive` or a call over the host socket cannot answer. A no writes the `history off` marker. The console says the first-run notice once after a yes (`history.notice`, console scope, marker `local/history.noticed`). `delphi history status` and `doctor` say "waiting for your answer" until then. **Not built:** the question at `delphi connect` (no workspace there), and a `delphi history on` for someone who said no (they delete `local/history.consent` and `local/history.off`, or ask again). Residual risks stay as S7 listed them: four redaction gaps, short pasted text kept as written, mission logs keep prompts after a purge, and nothing run live against a real terminal session. **Not a release:** no version was bumped. |

## Spec 103: the private-read mark (2026-11-12, approved)

Drafted at the operator's request (2026-11-12, "the span-mark egress design in parallel") to retire the four rows `specs/098` §12 item 16 still lists as `Unaudited`: Relay, Mail, Connector and Outbound. A task that reads a private entry gets a sticky mark; Outbound refuses, a Connector card gains a banner and always asks, and the relay stream carries a fixed phrase in place of agent-written text. It changes nothing about what flows in or about trust. Not approved; nothing is built. All hours are estimates.

| Slice | Content | Hours | State |
| :-- | :-- | --: | :-- |
| M0 | this spec, a Council review, amendments | 4-6 | spec approved 2026-11-12 (operator, chat; short answers in §7.1; pull request open, not merged); the Council review has not been run |
| M1 | the mark, the `READS` table and its test, log events, console clear, observe mode and counter; no send changes | 8-12 | built, draft PR (observe only: no read, prompt or send differs; `delphi-engine/src/private_read.rs`). The host's `search_kb` marks the host session for each private host-noted entry it returns (ids only; log event `task.private_read`); `READS` is a file-level inventory of the readers of entry text with what each does (`Marks`, `Withholds`, `NotYetMarked`, `NoReturn`) and a token-scan test; `delphi private-read [status \| clear <key>]` and the console methods `private_read.status` / `private_read.clear` (console scope only, logged as `task.private_read_cleared`); the counter (`local/private_read/counter.json`) counts marked tasks and how often an Outbound or Connector send would have changed, shown by `delphi doctor`. NOT built: the delegated clear (an agent holding an operator permission, OD-4: a decision before M2); any send change (M2, M3); the Relay and Mail observation (its seam is M3's); a mark from the three page readers (listed `NotYetMarked`: a writer or checker model with no tools and no path to the four channels); the console slash command. The `READS` scan is three accessors and is NOT validated complete (M4). Nothing ran against a live host. **Update 2026-11-13: why the counters read 0.** Nothing marks a Delphi task, and the one production path that marks a host session (`host.search_kb` returning a private noted entry) could not fire for a project-scoped host: its noted entries were never returned (see the spec 098 known gaps). With that fixed, a host that is handed a private noted entry marks its session; the counters count sends the mark would change or held, so they stay 0 until a marked session's task sends on a held channel. |
| M2 | Outbound ask and the Connector banner, always-ask and no relay approval; flips Connector and Outbound | 10-14 | built, draft PR (the operator asked for it on 2026-11-12; §7.1 OD-1 and OD-2: ask, not refuse). A `WebFetch`/`WebSearch` request (`harness_run.rs`) and a connector write, draft or text-bearing read (`connector_run.rs`) pass the task's record through the one egress check (`egress::check` with `Channel::Outbound` or `Channel::Connector`, the record from `private_read::send_record`); an unmarked task passes untouched and is byte-for-byte as before. When the check refuses (the task is marked, or its mark cannot be read: fail closed on these two channels only) the send becomes one Red card that carries a fixed banner and the exact request, is never covered by `allow similar`, and is approved only at the console (`merge_class::console_only` names `harness_outbound_marked` and a connector card carrying `private_read_mark`, so a relay reply can deny it and never approve it). Nothing is sent before an answer; a timeout is a denial. The key is `<mission>/<task>` (task ids repeat across missions). The sandbox proxy (`delphi-sandbox/src/egress.rs`) has a `Policy::held_by` flag that opens no tunnel while set; **no daemon code starts that proxy on main, so nothing sets the flag yet**. **Not built:** Relay and Mail (M3); the delegated clear (OD-4); child-task inheritance (OD-5, deferred, so a child does not inherit); the M4 red-team of the `READS` table. **Honest reach today:** `host.search_kb` marks the *host session* (`host-<session>`), and no Delphi span runs under that key, so nothing marks a Delphi task yet; M2's ask is built and tested but does not fire in production until a task-level reader marks (the three page readers are `NotYetMarked`). Never run live. |
| M3 | the relay-stream replacement; flips Relay and Mail | 8-12 | built, draft PR (the operator asked for it on 2026-11-12). The daemon filters every event before a relay client is sent it (`Hub::set_relay_filter`, installed by `private_read::install_relay_filter` when an engine is built): for a task whose mark is live, or cannot be read, or whose mission is marked, the agent-written fields of `approval.requested` (`summary`, `payload`, `pattern`), `decisions.review_requested` (each `summary` and `rationale`), `task.completed` (`reason`) and `task.failed` (`error`) become one fixed phrase, and the card carries `withheld: true`; ids, tier, tool, payload hash, status and counts stay. Mail is the relay's SMTP transport, so the same stream covers it. A task that read nothing private is sent exactly what it always was. The relay's `approval.list` agrees with its stream, and `approval.relay_resolve` refuses an allow for a withheld card (a deny stays open); the relay shows only Deny for it. The decision goes through `egress::check` with a record shaped from the mark (unreadable reads as private), so Relay and Mail are `Wired::Yes` (rows name `private_read.rs`); Connector and Outbound stay `Unaudited` until M2. NOT built: any agent-reachable clear (OD-4's delegated form), the Connector and Outbound behaviour (M2), a mark from the page readers. What marks a task today: only the host's `search_kb` marks the host session (M1); Delphi's own tasks read no private entry through a marking reader yet, so on a real install the filter changes nothing until a reader marks. Nothing ran against a real relay or mailbox. |
| M4 | a red-team pass, invariant map, live check | 4-6 | built, draft PR (2026-11-12, an independent pass by an agent that did not write M1 to M3). **Audited:** the reader table widened from 3 accessors to 11 (adds the raw `source.md` and `excerpts.json` files, the excerpt reader, the staged-note opt-in, the handoff reader, the indexed chunk reader and the history prompt text) and from 8 file rows to 21; two new name tables, `HOST_SURFACE` (all 24 host-socket methods) and `MCP_SURFACE` (all 39 MCP tools), fail on a method or tool that is not classified; the relay stream's event kinds and the relay socket's 5 methods are classified the same way; a fixture test proves the scan catches a new unclassified reader. **Result of the table:** of the 21 file rows: 1 marks (`host.search_kb`), 4 withhold by rule (claim slice twice, bundle export, history prompt), 4 `NotYetMarked` (the three page readers and the excerpt helper), 1 SKIPS (the handoff reader: another session's words reach a fresh unmarked session), 9 return no entry text (writers, counts, ids) and 2 serve only the operator's console. Of the 63 host and MCP names, 2 mark (one reader under two names), 2 skip (the same handoff reader), 59 write only or return no entry text. **Attacks (fake fixtures):** paraphrase (withheld with everything else a marked task wrote; the same words from an unmarked task are not: the mark is not content-based), child task (does not inherit: succeeds, OD-5), second MCP session of the same host (succeeds, own unit), mission-level mark against the Outbound and Connector check (succeeds: they read `<mission>/<task>` only; small exposure because host-started tasks are read-only at ceiling L0), key collisions and odd keys (over-hold only), a task that renames itself (no effect), a card with a damaged mark and no mission (**was fail-open, fixed**), a relay that reconnects after the card was raised with the mark on the mission (**was fail-open, fixed**: the engine now notes each raised card's mission), a clear mid-flight (the withheld card stays withheld, the next one is free), an Outbound ask nobody answers (denied), nothing reaches the clear but the console (and `local/` is never readable by a role). **Fixed:** the two relay fail-opens above (`private_read.rs`, one line in `engine.rs`). **Not fixed, for the operator:** the handoff reader, child and session inheritance (OD-5), the host mark not holding helper-task Outbound and Connector, the research job's web queries, the page and excerpt readers (kept `NotYetMarked` on purpose: marking a Delphi task would start the Outbound and Connector asks for research workflows), an index that lags a source made private. Invariant map (098 item 16) and spec 055 §10.1 reading updated. **Live check:** see the PR body (a scratch home and the real daemon binary; what could not run live is listed there). **Does M4 pass?** The relay and mail channels and the two acting senders do what M2 and M3 said for a marked task; the gaps are decisions and are listed. S8 is NOT marked ready and no default changed. |
| M5 | the quoting rule for `slack`, `email` and `private-doc` documents (§7.1 OD-6) | not estimated | design first; not started |
| M6 | OD-5: a child holds what its parent holds within a session (the operator, 2026-11-12: "the child should have the same scope as the parent in a given session") | 6-10 (estimate) | built, draft PR (stacked on nothing: main already carried M1 to M4). **Built:** one lookup, `private_read::task_scope`, used by the Outbound sender, the Connector sender and the relay filter (so Relay and Mail agree), over a task's own key, its recorded ancestors (at most 8 steps, a cycle ends the walk) and its session's key (a host session's mark, kept under `host-<session>`, now holds every task that session started on Outbound and Connector as well as on the relay, which M4 had pinned as a gap). **Links** between separate tasks are daemon-written (never a tool argument): the only such link Delphi creates is a timer's wake, noted when a role sets the timer and linked before the wake starts; an operator-set timer makes none; a task with no link behaves as before. **Counted:** `held_by_inheritance` (in `delphi doctor` and `private_read.status`) says how many holds came only by inheritance, which is what the rule costs research. **Fail-closed:** an unreadable mark or link on the chain holds the four channels, found by file name so a damaged record cannot hide. M4's two pinned non-inheritance tests are flipped on purpose; the different-session test stays. **Not done:** the handoff reader still skips the mark (not part of this decision); the page and excerpt readers stay `NotYetMarked` (so nothing in production marks a Delphi task yet and the new holds fire only for the host-session mark and for tasks whose mark a future reader sets); a card that names no mission and has none noted (a daemon restart after the card was raised) is found through the task id only, so a host-session mark is not found for it. Nothing ran against a live host. |


## Host experience program: specs 104 to 110 (2026-11-13, approved in advance)

The operator asked, in a host session on 2026-11-13, for a review of how Delphi's agents behave in a host, approved the plan that came out of it ("approve all your plans and suggestions and go") and asked for the work to be built locally first ("build as much locally as possible so we don't get slowed down by merges up front"). The seven specs were written after that approval, so each says in its frontmatter that the plan was approved and the text was not read. It was built on a local branch first and then split into pull requests, one spec at a time, on 2026-11-14.

| Spec | Slice | Content | State |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| 098 | S6 follow-up | the socket outlasts the daemon's wait: `ask_family` (630 s), `council_brief` and `council_status` (80 s), one wait table with a test | built (pull request open) |
| 104 | R0 | the spec | approved in advance 2026-11-13 |
| 104 | R1 | `ask_family` as a job, `ask_result`, identity, cancel | built (pull request open): the daemon starts the seat as a background job and hands back its `ask_id` at once (amendment §12 A1), the MCP process holds the tool call up to 45 s polling `ask_result`, which reads the answer once for its own session; `cancel_job` stops it, and the answer carries the strategist's identity |

## The desktop app: spec 111 (2026-11-14, approved on the plan)

The operator asked on 2026-11-14 whether a desktop app should replace the terminal's narrator, approved the plan and its defaults ("your default assumptions all sounds correct. go"), and asked for it to be built locally, slice by slice, before any pull request. ## The knowledge loop: specs 112 to 119 (2026-11-14, approved)

The operator asked on 2026-11-14 for an audit of the scout, the investigator and the knowledge base against one goal: structured research that builds a store of knowledge for agents and operators alike. The audit read the code on `main` and one live workspace (read-only) and made eight recommendations. The operator asked for specs covering all eight, with slices and hours ("make specs covering all 8, show me LOE and slices"), was shown the drafts' slice tables and open decisions, and answered the same day: "115: your suggestions are approved. 119: let's make excerpts the default between pointers and full private text. otherwise. 116: on by default, and empower the investigator however you can. 117: for the desktop app drawings are better than descriptions but should not be required as descriptions are adequate", then "all other specs are approved". The operator saw the slice tables and the decisions, not every line of the texts; 116 §8 was rewritten after their word and has not been read. Nothing is built. Later the same day the operator settled three open items: claims from private and internal sources are shared across projects under three guards ("1. follow your direction"); Brave and Mojeek as engines, with the host's own search tools added ahead of them ("your choices sound good, but also add the host default tools as well, like claud's ewb search in a claude environment"); and an app scope paired from a terminal by a key exchange, started narrow and widened explicitly, which is spec 120, approved the same day ("approve 120. add to the build plan"). 119 §2.1 and the changes to 115 §2 item 3 were written after those words and have not been read. Build order and hours by spec are in spec 119 §7 (359 to 474 hours, spec 120 not counted). The first builder stopped on 2026-11-15 with 32 of about 64 slices built; what was left, in order, and how to finish it were in a handoff file beside this tracker, deleted on 2026-11-16 in the pull request of the last slice (119 B6). The rows below are the record.

| Spec | Slice | Content | State |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |

No live network run was made |

| 112 | R2 | run claims become library claims, merged by fingerprint; `delphi research migrate` | built and merged (on `main` by #724, which landed the stack; built in #709): an anchor on a source kept as a pointer holds the quote alone (at most 400 characters, `via: research`), because the library's ordinary anchor needs kept text; a claim scoped to another project is neither merged nor replaced (spec 115 decides); a claim the library already holds is in `library_claims`, not in the run's `claims` |

Read: with this model the method costs about half as many tokens again for fewer claims, because the plan and answer calls add calls and the claim call (cap 1,500) reached its cap on 40 to 50% of calls on both sides (26 of 54 before, 37 of 93 after), so claims were lost to cut-off replies; nothing here measures the method with the model it is meant for. | 116 | G9 | the investigator's shell in a throwaway worktree: the manifest, the worktree's life, the unchanged-project test, the refusals | built and merged (#742), for the red-team before merge: the investigator's manifest gains `shell-worktree-scoped` and `git-worktree`. Its `run_command` and `shell` run in a copy of the project made at the span's first command and deleted when the span ends (a guard that deletes on any exit, and a sweep of leftovers). Found while building, and different from the spec's words: the copy is a repository of its own (`git init`, then the daemon fetches the project's branches, tags and head into it and checks out detached), not a linked worktree, because a linked worktree keeps its index, refs and new objects inside the project's `.git`, and the sandbox refuses every write to a `.git` path; the copy's git directory is beside its files under another name. It has no remote. Commands ALWAYS run in the OS sandbox with no network, also when `[exec] sandbox` is off (a builder's then run unsandboxed); with no sandbox on the machine the tools are not offered. Cards, deny rules, the Hard class and the taint gate are the builder's code path; push, fetch, pull, clone, remote and submodule are also refused by name. `builds` (scope and an `@worktree/` write glob) now decides everything that used to read the scope alone: a task's worktree, a harness CLI's own tools, the route's approval bridge, and an operator authorization (spec 092), none of which the investigator gets. Tests hold the whole project directory byte-identical (`.git` included) after a bisect (AC-10) and after a push, a network request and writes outside (AC-11). Limits: the copy costs a full fetch of the project's history; on macOS `/usr/bin/git` is a shim that does not run in the sandbox, so `[exec] toolchain` and `PATH` must name a real git; a span already untrusted (it read research or web text) is refused the shell as a builder would be; a harness seat without the tool bridge has no shell |

For the operator: whether a span that is already untrusted may still use `verify_source` on a trusted host without a card (built: yes; since §14, a span that has run a command is asked), and whether it may use the sandboxed shell (built: no) |

| 117 | E1 | topic pages: slug ids, rebuilt in place, versions listed, the change line, due and idle rebuild | built and merged (#733): a topic is `library/pages/<slug>.md` with a `topic_page` sidecar, made from each non-private research topic (the idle look makes them) or by `delphi pages new` (refused inside an agent seat); a slug that could name a search page's key, a mechanical page's file or a folder of `library/pages/` is refused. A rebuild uses the search page's writer, check and validator, moves the page it replaces and its record to `versions/<slug>/<stamp>`, keeps at most 50, and stores a change line counted by code (statements are cited paragraphs, compared without their markers; one cited record removed and one added is "replaced"); the line is in the record, not in the file, so the stored page still passes `delphi pages check`. Due: never built, a claim or source it was built from changed or left, a research topic it follows gained a live claim, its lifetime passed. The daemon rebuilds one due page every 10 minutes while the operator has been quiet 5 minutes and no task runs, within `per_day`; `[library.pages] auto_rebuild`, default **true** as §2 item 3 reads (the spec's open question 1 is the operator's). Topic pages are built at the external scope: no private record reaches the writer or the page (`[library] private_to_models = deny`); the app reads private sources through `library.source`. Not built: a topic made by the console's search when none matches, and a search that matches a topic opening it (§2 items 1 and 7). The operator, 2026-11-15, on idle rebuild: "on by default for now but make a note to revisit the idea of a y/n question on setup": to revisit, a yes/no question about idle rebuild in `delphi init` or setup |
| 117 | E2 | gather through the retrieval service over the whole corpus; `s-kb` and finding records | built and merged (#733): one read-only search of the workspace index (the operator's view, untrusted included, no reranker) per build; a library source's hit joins the page's sources, a library claim's joins its claims, a research answer or other passage becomes `[s-kb-<16 hex>]`, a finding `[f-<16 hex>]`, at most 8; labels hash the passage's place, the record keeps a hash of its text, and a changed or vanished passage makes the page due (checked only when the index is opened: the idle look and the CLI, never the app's reads). Left out: staged, episodes, provisional notes, topic record JSON, another project's hits, and hits flagged `internal`. The check resolves those markers only against the records the build was given |

Sections are stored on the topic's record for the app. The link table (`library/pages/links.json`) is rewritten at every build |
