They are **not** narrator methods: the narrator list is unchanged, so a private source's text reaches the app only on a paired key, and no gate file changed. A test calls each through the dispatcher at the app scope on a workspace holding a public and a private source, claims, a research topic, a twice-built topic page, a finding, lessons and an index, and finds every file and folder byte-identical and no model called. Reads never open the index (a passage's change is not shown as due there). Not done: the app's bridge list (`Method::ALL`, its pins and the window's calls) is unchanged; E5 adds the bridge methods, and its pin must then allow read-grant methods that are sent only on the paired socket (or the narrator list must grow, which is a gate-file change in `service_gates_tests.rs`); `library.source` returns a kept source's whole text, since 119 §3 (private text kept for retrieval) is not built |
| 117 | E5 | app: encyclopedia home and the topic page with the citation panel and source highlight | built and merged (#739): two menu items, Encyclopedia and Agent memory. **The bridge:** `client::Method` holds twenty reads, the nine narrator methods and the encyclopedia's eleven (`Method::PAIRED_ONLY`); the client refuses a paired-only read on any connection that did not prove a key, before a byte is written, so the narrator socket is never asked and the narrator list and the gate file are untouched. The pin `every_method_the_app_can_send_is_on_the_daemons_narrator_allowlist` now holds each method to exactly one of the two lists, the paired-only list equal to the daemon's `ENCYCLOPEDIA_READS` in its order, and all twenty under the `read` grant; `an_encyclopedia_read_is_sent_only_on_a_paired_connection` runs both sockets. The window names a read and hands over an object; `ency.rs` builds the parameters from its known fields, each checked for shape. A window that is not paired is told to run `delphi app`. **Chose (no drawing was needed; the screens are as §4 describes them):** the home page finds topics by title, key term or open question as you type and hands the words to the knowledge-base search on enter; topics by letter or by project (a topic follows its research topic's project); a topic page shows the daemon's banner, what changed, the lead, key terms, a contents list made from the page's own headings, the sections, then bibliography, start here, see also, what links here, open questions and versions; a marker is a button that opens a side panel beside the page; a source's text is drawn as text, never as Markdown, with the quotation marked where it is found with spaces folded (AC-4). **Found:** `pages.get` replaces `built` (a time) with a yes or no, so the app reads the build time from `pages.list` and `pages.versions`; a source kept under 119 §3 (`kept_privately`) is sent whole by `library.source`, so the app's bridge cuts it to the cited passage before the window is given it (§3 item 3), and the daemon should do so itself. Not run against a real daemon or in the built app's WebKit view: the window's tests run on a fixture that began as the daemon's own answers (`app/src/fixture/ency.json`), and the screens were looked at in headless Chrome |

The operator is shown notes and findings made inside private projects, labelled (AC-7; 115 §11). **Chose:** episodes are not in `memory.role`'s answer, so they are read through the knowledge-base listing the app already has (`/v1/documents?collection=episodes`), by the role its front matter or its folder names; lesson files carry no date, so a lesson is in the dated list only when its line states one |
| 117 | E8 | app: read state, annotations, pins, export | built and merged (#739): `<home>/app/marks.json`, mode 0600 in the app's folder of mode 0700, one JSON object of at most 4 MiB, never given to a `Client` (§5); reading it creates nothing, a file this app did not write or a link in its place is left alone, and a window that could not read its marks does not write. A version is marked read when its end comes into view; the home page lists a page as new until then and as changed after a rebuild (AC-6). A note is attached to a paragraph, list or quote by that passage's words without its markers, so it is carried to any version that keeps the words and listed as orphaned, with the words, when none does (AC-9). Not verified: printing and showing the export in the Finder in the built app |

| 118 | P1 | the licence record on every asset; refusal without one; `assets check`; the daemon's credit line and its check | built and merged (#741): the record lives in the source's `images` (file, source id, the picture's address, licence, `licence_evidence` naming which check decided it, `commons`, `figure_link`, `host_entry`, `workspace` or `bundle`, and where; author, credit address, share-alike, retrieved, blake3, width, height). Written by the runner, page expansion (which threw it away: it now goes through the runner's own picture path, so the same gates apply, alt text included), a workspace document's capture (`internal`) and `bundle add` (the bundle's record only when its licence passes the picture decision, else the source's licence when that passes or is `own`/`internal`; anything else stays unrecorded). The image method answers 403 `no licence record`, and sends the credit with a picture it serves; a page may not show an unrecorded picture. `delphi library assets check [--apply] [--json]` (new top-level command) moves to `assets/unlicensed/`, never served, deletes nothing, refused in an agent seat. The credit line (`*Picture credit: author, licence, credit address*`) is written by `finish_page` (template 3); a writer's own is refused, and `pages check` fails a figure without the exact line. A picture record's addresses are not links a page may carry. The app's bridge passes `src=lib:<source>/<file>` (plus `max`, nothing else), rebuilt from plain parts. The run's record lists each picture kept (source, file, licence) or refused with a fixed word chosen by code, never the page's words. `record_research` takes `sources[].pictures` (at most 6 https addresses): the daemon judges only those, on the page it read and kept whole, on its own transport inside `[research.limits]`; a name the page does not show is `not_on_page`, a page kept as a pointer gives `text_not_kept`; a private source's pictures are never judged, an address with a privacy identifier is dropped. Not proven live: no capture from a real trusted host was looked at in the app (§7 asks for one) |

| 118 | P4 | WebP; SVG rendered safely at capture | built and merged (#747, on the operator's word of 2026-11-16; the red-team's review of the hostile-SVG fixtures had been asked for before merge): SVG capture is off by default (`[research] svg = false`, §11 decision 1; an SVG is refused `svg_off` before it is parsed) until a child-process render backstop lands in a later gate pull request; WebP is on. two new dependencies, `image-webp` 0.2.4 (MIT OR Apache-2.0, in `delphi-docs`) and `resvg` 0.50 with no default features (Apache-2.0 OR MIT; brings `usvg`, `roxmltree`, `tiny-skia` (BSD-3-Clause), `svgtypes`, `kurbo`, `simplecss` and small crates, every licence on `deny.toml`'s list; `cargo deny` not run locally). A WebP is kept as it is (`.webp`), its canvas and every frame's own header checked against the canvas and 24 million pixels before decoding, and served by the image method with `max`; the console decodes it too. An SVG is sniffed by its start, drawn at capture to a PNG of at most 1,600 pixels a side by `research_svg`, and kept beside the PNG as `<its own hash>.svg`, named in the PNG's record (`rendered_from`); an SVG never has a record of its own (`is_record_of` refuses one), so nothing serves it, and `assets check` does not list it. Bounds: 2 MiB, no DTD, 200,000 XML nodes, 64 levels, 50,000 elements and 1,000,000 path segments once `use` and markers are counted where drawn (every shape's segments, read with `svgtypes`, wherever it is drawn, and times every marker; a loop refused; on the built tree at most 3,000,000 points), every reference read as usvg reads it (`svgtypes` IRI and FuncIRI, `href` before `xlink:href`), no two elements of one id, clip-path chains at most 4 links and no marker inside a marker (both followed through `use`), at most 64 stops a gradient, no style sheet that sets either, no `pattern`, `filter` or `mask`, at most 256 layers nested 4 deep (each clip of a chain a layer) and none over two canvases, painting of at most 200 canvases (a gradient weighs 3 plus one per 8 stops) and 200,000 dashes, one drawing at a time in the process (a drawing past 10 seconds is refused but keeps the slot until it ends, and a caller behind it is refused at once), the drawing thread's stack 256 MB of address space. Three red-team passes (2026-11-16) found the clip-chain abort and 2 GB, the marker-count bypasses, the unbounded CPU after a timeout, the WebP frame size, the SVG's name, then a clip chain through `use` and gradients with many stops, then long paths drawn many times through `use` (4.7 GB) and references spelled so the audit and usvg read them differently; each is fixed with a test. No hard memory ceiling on a drawing is possible inside the daemon (an allocation failure in usvg or tiny-skia aborts the process); a real backstop needs the drawing in a child process with memory and CPU limits, which is a new process-start site (a `spawn_sites` gate change, the operator's). A record's addresses now keep their `_` (P1's `one_line` had broken Wikipedia addresses in credit lines). Every `<image>` reference is refused by our resolver; no resources folder, no text, no fonts. The licence decision is unchanged and made before any byte is fetched. Found: an SVG with text is refused (`svg_not_drawn`), because drawing it without its words would mislead and drawing text needs fonts; most diagrams on the open web have text, so the operator should decide whether to draw text with fonts the daemon ships or reads from the system. Not verified: no real SVG from a trusted host was captured; the app's bridge change (`.webp` in the library form) was not run (`app/` is outside CI) |
| 118 | P5 | figures in pages: the figure spec, rendering, the label, the number and label check | built and merged (#748): the writer asks for a figure with a fenced `figure` block holding one JSON object in the 095 chart or diagram form (`type`, `title`, inline `data` rows or `nodes`/`edges`) plus `from`, the claim or source markers it rests on (at most 20, at most 6 figures a page; `data.from` files and a `source` line are refused). The daemon resolves the markers at the page's scope (a private one fails an external page), renders with the existing `delphi_docs` chart and diagram code to PNG, stores it in `library/figures/` with a record (`licence: "generated"`, `made_by` the writer's role ID, `markers`, kind, title, blake3, width, height) and puts it in the page as a picture at `lib:_figures/` plus the hash; `finish_page` writes `Figure made by <role> from [markers]. Not from a source.` under it, a writer's own such line is refused and `pages check` fails a figure without the exact line. The check: every number in a chart's data and in the text it draws (title, subtitle, column names, annotations) must equal a whole number token of a cited claim's text or a cited source's stored text (so `1` is never found inside `2019`); every diagram node label must appear as whole words in the page text outside figure blocks. A failing figure is dropped, the failure (kind, title, reason, one plain line) goes to the page sidecar, the validator verdict and the `figure_dropped` log entry, and `pages show` prints it. A `lib:_figures/` address the writer typed is dropped, so a figure is never reused unchecked on another page. The image method serves a figure with its record and label instead of a picture credit; `assets check` covers `figures/`. First draft by Gemini via agy, reviewed and revised. Left: figures are not carried by `library export` (per-source); figures of a draft that later failed stay on disk with their records; a diagram's edge labels and sequence messages are not checked (§5 item 3 names node labels); "add to a topic" for mission artifacts (§5 item 5) |

The guard is applied in the host runner, the console job and the validator's searcher; handing queries to a host is a seam (B7) |

| 119 | B2 | `[library] unknown_licence` (default `excerpts`), private text, the 300-character rule and its scan test, purge, list, the cap | built and merged (#734): a research run keeps each passage it quoted from a page it does not hold whole (at most 300 characters, up to 200 either side) as an excerpt, and the claim's anchor is an ordinary one into it; the passages (never their context) are indexed as `library/sources/<id>/excerpts.md`. **Anchors already written** (112 R2, quote alone, `via: research`) stay as they are and readable; the next time a run reads that page under `excerpts`, every claim citing it whose quote is on the page gains the passage and the anchor is replaced in place (nothing is fetched for this alone; a recheck does not do it). The library's 10% bound is not applied to these passages (10% of a short page is shorter than one quote); 16 KiB a source is, counted with the context. `private_text` keeps such a page whole as a private source marked `kept_privately`, unless it shows a rights notice the code detects or its trusted-list entry allows a summary only (§3 item 3 says `reuse = none`; the list's word is `summary-only`); a page may carry at most 300 characters of it in one run (a page-check finding over the whole body, for cited `kept_privately` sources); its bibliography says `kept privately`; the cap removes the text last read or checked longest ago (no read is recorded, so `last_checked`). Found while building: a `kept_privately` source counts as no origin and its claims are withheld from shared search, as every private source is. **Not built: an inside agent's search does not reach private text** (AC-4's first half): the index lists no private source and `[library] private_to_models = deny` keeps private entries out of prompts, so reaching it needs the private-read mark at retrieval and the operator's word. Purge and list are `delphi sources private list\|purge`, not `delphi library private` (there is no `library` command; the library's commands are under `sources`). `[library] private_to_models` now parses as a key (it was refused as an unknown field) and the config schema's two `library` objects are one |
| 119 | B3 | the defaults of §4 offered by setup; a run asks for an archived copy | built and merged (#734): `delphi research setup` asks one yes or no each, at a terminal, about retraction lookup, archived copies, citation chaining and background validation, each with what it sends or spends; the default answer is what `config.toml` says now (which are on is the operator's, §12 item 2) and only a change is written. Not offered: session summaries for host sessions (no setting exists; 113 H6), and chaining for `deep` runs only (the setting is on or off). A run that finds a page gone (404 or 410) that a claim rests on asks the archive for a copy, only with `archive_fallback` on; the copy is not read by the run and adds no origin |
| 119 | B5 | the small faults of §6 | built and merged (#734): each claim of a result carries its sources' licence and capture level (`sources`), `freshness.last_checked` is the time of the check, `budget_used` gains `ms`; the validator's charter no longer says the fetcher is not built. Already fixed, not redone: `budget_used.check_steps` (112 R3); the answer's label (114 M5); `want` and `context_pointers` were already absent from `request_research`'s schema, which refuses unknown keys (a test now pins it); the default depth is `survey` in the code, the tool schema and the docs (only spec 066's own text says `quick`, which needs an amendment). Not done: spec 052's frontmatter, an approved spec's text |
| 119 | B9 | never refused, local runs, Parallel then Brave (§13.2, §13.3), with §15 | built and merged (#744): a `request_research` or `/research` with `providers = []` is queued as a `local` run instead of refused: it is handed no engine, sends no query, and reads the addresses Delphi already knew for the question that are on the trusted list (from the library when held fresh, else by the daemon's fetcher); the reply, the result and the record say `sources: engines | local` and `web_search`, and the answer's label ends `, no web search` whenever no search answered (an engine run whose every search failed says so too). `research-asks.jsonl` writes `no_web_search` (an old `no_engine` line still counts as asked and not run); the brief's sentence, the doctor's warning and `kb health` (`research_30d.no_web_search`) follow that count. `delphi init` writes `[search] providers = ["parallel"]` (the example config has it uncommented), so init's last line no longer has a missing engine to point at; `research setup` offers Parallel first and Brave second, a Brave key gives `["parallel", "brave"]` (Parallel stays first where it is first; a table the operator emptied, or one that never had Parallel, does not gain it), and Parallel's notice (retention terms not published) is printed before any query, also when Brave is the engine added. The validator's search (foreground and background, §15 item 6) asks Parallel only with `[validator] background = true`; an engine set up with a key or an address is asked as before. By §15 a session or task under the private-read mark is **not** made local: its run is the same, each query cleaned and through the identifier guard, and the reply (`planned_under_mark`, `mark_note`), the result and the record say it was planned under the mark; a local run still chains citations when `citation_chaining` is on (a test). Not done: `sources: host` (B7); the mark of an inside agent's task is looked up by its mission id, which is how the daemon keys it elsewhere, and no test covers that path |
| 119 | B10 | the defaults and the 10 KB rule (§14 items 2 and 3) | built and merged (#744): `boot::settings_from` turns retraction lookup, the archived copy and citation chaining on for every workspace whose config does not set them (`retractions`, `archive_fallback`, `citation_chaining` under `[research]`; `false` turns each off, a value of the wrong kind keeps it on); `Settings::default()` leaves all three off, so an engine built with no config asks neither the archive nor the citation index. `research setup` asks about background validation only (off unless answered yes) and says in one line which three are on and the keys that turn them off. The doctor reports a retraction check with no data imported as a fact when the key was never set and as a warning when the operator set it. The library's 10% bound applies only to a source whose recorded length is over 10,240 bytes (`library_excerpts::total_allowed`); a shorter one has the 16 KiB cap alone. The same rule now holds a research run's passages: for a source over 10 KB the quotes kept (counted as an operator's excerpts are, without their context) may not pass a tenth of it; a source whose length is not recorded has the passage's bounds and the 16 KiB cap. Not measured: what the three defaults cost a real run (requests to archive.org and OpenCitations); nothing here was run against the network |
| 119 | B7 | the host as a search source (§2.1 items 1 to 7, §13.1) | built and merged (#744): in a host whose adapter names a search tool (the host tool table is `mcp_hosts::host_search_tools`, read from the adapters of spec 110: Claude Code only today), `request_research` queues a run with `sources: host` and answers with `host_search: "asked"`, the host's tool name and the queries the run planned, each cleaned and through the identifier guard (it waits up to 25 seconds for the plan; `research_status` lists them under `host_search` when the plan takes longer); `runner: "daemon"` asks for Delphi's own runner. The new host method and MCP tool `research_feed {research_id, query, results[]}` is taken only from the session that raised the run, only for a query the run asked, exactly as asked, once each, with at most 10 http(s) addresses, at most 12 queries, and only while the run waits (240 seconds, a constant; `HOST_METHODS` is 32, `HOST_SURFACE` 32, `MCP_SURFACE` 46, each with the new row). Only a result's address is kept: titles and snippets are dropped at the socket. The run reads a fed address that is on the trusted list through the daemon's fetcher; any other is never fetched (it is counted in the log as `lead` and listed in the result as `host_leads`), is not stored as a library lead, and never enters the set of addresses a search of the run returned. Under the private-read mark a feed is leads only (as `record_research` fetches nothing under it, 113 §2 item 5): an operator question, since §15 says a marked session researches as any other. Each fed search is a log line marked `run_by_host` with source `host:<tool>`. With engines configured, the queries the host did not hand back, and the later rounds for sub-questions still uncovered, go to the engines; with `providers = []` the run ends after the host's turn (`engines: none` of §2.1 item 6 is said as `sources: host` with no engine line in the log). Found while building: the existing socket tests of the daemon's runner use the tool name `claude-code`, so they now pass `runner: "daemon"`; the B9 local-run test moved to a host with no search tool (Codex). Not done: no real host has run the loop; the 240-second wait and the 25-second plan wait are guesses; a host row beyond Claude Code's (Copilot lists a fetch tool and no search tool; Antigravity and Gemini CLI have no adapter) |
| 119 | B8 | the host tool table; search and fetch tools in the scout's and the investigator's worker files (§2.1 items 2 and 8, AC-2c) | built and merged (#744): the table is `mcp_hosts::host_search_tools`, read from each host's adapter row (`search_web`, `fetch`), so the two cannot disagree; a host with no adapter has neither. The scout's and the investigator's Claude Code worker files list `WebSearch` and `WebFetch` after the read tools (`worker_host_tools`); no other role's file does, and the files of Codex, Antigravity and Copilot name neither. `ecosystem/` regenerated. Naming a tool grants nothing: the host's permission rules decide each call. Not done: Copilot's fetch tool is in the table and not in its worker files (the alias a Copilot agent file uses for it was not checked live) |
| 119 | B8 (Copilot) | Copilot's fetch tool in its worker files (§16 item 3) | built (pull request open): checked live with the Copilot CLI signed in on the build machine (`--version` 1.0.108): an agent file lists the fetch tool by its own name, `web_fetch` (the alias `web` gives the same tool; a file without either has no fetch tool), and a listed `web_fetch` ran once against an allowed address. `copilot_tools` now takes the host's tools from `worker_host_tools`, so the scout's Copilot worker file lists `"read", "search", "web_fetch"` before its Delphi tools; the investigator's has no tools line since 116 G11 and inherits the session's; no other role's names it. The B8 test pins this for Copilot instead of refusing it, and the spec 110 test of Copilot's tool lines allows it for the scout only. `ecosystem/` holds Claude Code's files only and did not change; Copilot's files are written by `delphi plugin export --host copilot`, and the exported scout file was checked live too. Six Copilot requests in all |
| 119 | B6 | the live proof (on Parallel, §15 item 7) | run live in part, 2026-11-16 (pull request open); the model half did not run. **How:** a scratch home (a temporary `--home`, never `~/.delphi`), `delphi init`, `delphi setup` (it found Claude Code on a Max plan, Antigravity, Codex, Copilot and Ollama; the change was ratified in the scratch home), Parallel's keyless engine as `[search]` (§15 item 7: no key), and runs raised as a host through `delphi mcp`'s `request_research` with `runner: "daemon"`. **What ran live:** five runs on two public questions (two `quick`, three `survey`), about eight Parallel searches, every one answered with 10 results; the daemon's fetcher read six BBC pages and refused nine addresses (every Britannica page answers it 403, one AP News page failed, one `http://` address was not fetchable); topic records, search logs and six library sources, each a pointer with no text, were written. **What did not run, and why:** no claim was kept, so no topic page was built, no person read one, and a session in a second registered project found no research (its `search_kb` returned none, as expected with no claim); the `deep` run was not started. The daemon's scout calls are single turns (`scout_key` asks `turn_by_turn`), and routing never gives a single turn to a harness seat ("a harness runs whole spans, not single turns"), so of the five endpoints setup connected only Ollama could answer; it was serving the concurrent 114 M7 evaluation, and a five-token test call did not return in two minutes. There is no model key on this machine, so this part stopped here, as the builder was told to. **Found, for the operator:** (1) on a machine with only subscription seats, a run on the one runner (the console's `/research`, an inside agent's request, a host's `request_research`) has no cloud model to read its pages with, and nothing says so: before the workspace had any model, runs ended in seconds as `done` with "No claim could be drawn", not as failed (a host's `record_research`, where the host's own model reads the pages, needs no daemon model); (2) a run's id restarts at `r-t_0001` in every MCP session and the topic record keys runs by it, so three runs on one topic from three sessions show as one run whose lines overwrite each other; (3) a run in flight when the daemon stops stays `running` in its topic record; (4) the trusted query names only the first five hosts of the trusted list (`anthropic.com`, `apnews.com`, `arxiv.org`, `bbc.com`, `britannica.com`: `plan_topic` keeps the first chunk of `site_queries`), so Wikipedia, `*.gov` and PubMed are never named, and a `quick` run (5 steps) spent its budget on Britannica's refusals and never sent the topic query; (5) `delphi setup` run outside a registered project sets up the `default` workspace, not the one `delphi init --project` made. None of these is fixed here. **Spend:** the Parallel searches (keyless), one Claude endpoint probe (7,438 tokens, through the subscription), no other cloud model call; the local model's calls were counted by nobody |

Checked on macOS 27.0.1 with two ad-hoc-signed builds: a build reads its own item with no prompt; a different build cannot read or delete it and can write over it; so a rebuilt or updated unsigned app cannot read its key until the operator allows it in the system's dialog. | 120 | K4 | the `request` grant: four methods, ids and typed text only, limits, events; the gate file's check | built and merged (#743) except the gate file itself: `app.research_continue {topic_id, section?, note?}`, `app.research_ask {question, follows?}`, `app.page_rebuild {slug}`, `app.page_dispute {claim, note}` are the `request` row of `app_keys::GRANTS` (27 methods in all). The server hands them to the engine through an entry of their own (`Extension::app_request`, with the session's key name), only for an app session whose key holds the grant at that call; the engine's ordinary entry never sees them, so the console, narrator, relay and host sockets answer "unknown method". **Revocation (found in K1):** the server runs each request as a task of its own, so a key revoked mid-request closes the session at once and the request still runs to its own end; a research run is queued in the one queue and a rebuild is its own task, so no work is held inside a call. Each runs the code of the terminal's command as the operator's (`continue_as_operator`, `launch_research`, `topic_rebuild`, `dispute_claim`). A parameter outside the method's list, or one that is not a string, refuses the call before anything is counted. **Chose:** limits are per key, 30 an hour counted for every well-formed request whether or not the daemon then starts it, 4 running at once, kept in memory (a restart forgets the hour); a request past a limit writes no event, so the log cannot be flooded; `app.request` is written again at each change of state (`queued`, `running`, `done`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `refused` with the daemon's sentence) and never holds typed text; a section is named in the run's note as `(section: <anchor>)` after the daemon finds a heading with that anchor on a page that follows the topic, so the one page-derived thing a request carries is at most 80 lowercase letters, digits and hyphens; a follow-up question is researched in the project of the topic it follows and the id it follows is recorded only in the event; a rebuild already running for the app is not started twice. **Found:** the 2,000-character bound of §4.2 item 5 is never the binding one, since the terminal's own rules still apply (a note 120 characters, a question 200, a dispute 500); the console allows one research run at a time, so a second research request while one runs is refused by that rule, not queued; AC-8 is shown only as "no request method answers a card and the app scope is refused `approval.resolve`", since no run of these four raises a card today. **The gate file** (`crates/delphi/tests/app_grants.toml`) and its line in `merge-gates.toml` are gate paths and are in a pull request of their own for the operator to merge at a terminal; until then `crates/delphi/tests/app_grants.rs` holds the table to a copy in the test, and once `merge-gates.toml` names the file its absence or any difference fails |
| 120 | K5 | the app: buttons in place of the clipboard, the paired state in the title row, request states | built and merged (#743): **the bridge** gains the four requests (`client::Method::REQUESTS`, 24 methods: 20 reads and these), sent only on a connection that proved a key (the client refuses them on the narrator socket before a byte is written) and only when the key held `request` when its session opened (otherwise the window is told which command gives it, and the daemon is not asked); `ask.rs` builds each request's parameters from the fields that request takes, each checked for shape, and passes on nothing else the window hands over; the pin `every_method_the_app_can_send_is_on_the_daemons_narrator_allowlist` now holds every method to exactly one of narrator read, paired-only read or request, and the requests equal to the daemon's `REQUEST_METHODS` in its order. **The window:** in a window that may ask, "Rebuild now" and "Run it again" are buttons, and "Ask a follow-up", "Research this section further" and "Dispute this statement" open one line and send it; otherwise each is the clipboard command of 117 E9, which now also says how to pair. **Chose:** "Ask a follow-up" sends `app.research_ask` (the question, and the topic it follows) where the clipboard command is `research continue --note`; "Research this section further" sends the topic's id, the section's anchor and the typed words, never the heading (the clipboard command still quotes the heading, which the operator reads before running it); a test reads the window's source and fails when a request is made from anything but an id, a slug, an anchor or the field's own words, or when anything but the app's frame sends one. The title row says `read-only` or `paired as <name>` with the grants held, and beside `read-only` why a key was not accepted (`Conn.unpaired_because`); until now a connected window said `read-only` whatever it was and showed no reason. A request's line says asking, queued, running, done, failed or refused with the daemon's sentence, taken from the `app.request` events the window already reads; "waiting on a card elsewhere" is said while a request is queued or running and any decision waits on the operator (the events do not say which run a card belongs to). Nothing in it moves: no spinner, no transition, and a test holds the controls' styles to that. Not verified: the buttons in the built app's WebKit view against a real daemon (the window's tests are static renders and pure functions; the bridge's run against a socket the test serves) |
| 120 | K6 | the red-team fixtures and the end-to-end check | built and merged (#743) as far as an agent can take it: **the fixtures** are tests. A document that tries to cause a request: a source, a claim, a topic page and a heading whose text is a request, an instruction and a button are read by every read the app has, and nothing is asked, counted or started; a request carrying a document's words in place of an id, or beside one, is refused before it is counted; the window draws the same text as text, with no button, script or link to a method made from it (`redteam_a_document_that_tries_to_cause_a_request_causes_none`, and `app/test/request.test.tsx`). A second program on the app socket and a replayed handshake: against the daemon's real server and engine, a request sent before a handshake ends the connection, a signature by another key or a recorded hello sent again is refused and recorded, and none disputes anything (`redteam_a_paired_key_asks_end_to_end_and_a_stranger_or_a_replay_asks_for_nothing`). **The end-to-end check** is that same test for the daemon's half (a ticket, pairing, a session on the key alone, a request, its event arriving on the session, revocation closing it) and the bridge's tests for the app's half, each against the other side's stand-in. **Not done: the check on a machine** (AC-1: pair from a terminal, quit, start from the Finder, ask a follow-up, revoke). A ticket is given only to a client at a terminal and an agent has none, by design; `delphi app` does not start the app until its process-start site is listed (K2's own pull request); and the window's buttons were not clicked in the built app. `app/README.md` has the five steps for a person, with `pair_probe` (which can now ask, `DELPHI_PROBE_ASK`) or the window. **Found for the red-team:** a connection that sends anything but a handshake first is closed without an `app.refused` record (only a wrong ticket, signature or key is recorded), so a program probing the socket that way is not counted toward the lockout; the anchor of a heading reaches a research run's note as the operator's words (at most 80 lowercase letters, digits and hyphens); the hour's count of requests is forgotten when the daemon restarts |

K4's gate file and its line in `merge-gates.toml` are merged (#745). Also not done: the end-to-end check on a machine by a person, and the buttons in the built app; the open questions of §11; a record for a connection closed before any handshake. Whether the red-team reviewed K1 and K4 before they merged (§9) is not recorded here |

## Data sensitivity: spec 121 (2026-11-15, draft)

The operator asked on 2026-11-15 to clarify the private and internal concepts and to add confidential. A Panel (council t_0001) ruled PROCEED-WITH-CHANGES; the draft is built on its consensus. A draft is a proposal: nothing may be built against it, and the text of specs 098, 103, 113, 115 and 119 that it would narrow (its section 11) is unchanged until it is approved.

| Spec | Slice | Content | State |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |

| 121 | S0 (pointers) | the pointer lines §11 says the approving pull request adds to the specs it narrows | built (pull request open): one line at each place §11 names, and no other change to those specs: 103 §2 (the paragraph on KB kinds), 103 §7.1 (rows 6 and 7) and the M5 paragraph; 113 §2 (item 4); 115 §2 (item 3) and 115 §11.2; 119 §3 and 119 §14 (item 1, unchanged, pointing at §16 question 1); 098 §7.2 (unchanged, pointing at §16 question 3). `compiled/` regenerated |
| 121 | S1 | the vocabulary and the daemon's record; `[handling]`; assignment by path, marking and operator; add-only; propagation on writes; `delphi handling status`, `set`, `clear`, `reconcile`; the doctor line; the separation tests | built (pull request open): `delphi_engine::handling` keeps the record in `<ws>/local/handling/records.json` (workspace items, by workspace path) and `<home>/library/handling/records.json` (library sources and claims), each trait with who set it (`path` with `configured`, `marking`, `operator`, `agent:<role>`, `host`, `derived` with the ids it came from) and the content hash; a frontmatter line `handling: [...]` (a claim's `handling` field) is a view, put back at each write and by `reconcile`. `[handling]` (`org_domains`, `list_addresses`, `company_wide_recipients`, `[[handling.path]]` kind/under/traits; a broken table configures nothing). The built-in defaults of §3 item 1 carry `by: path, configured: false`: they label and do not weigh, and do not let a marking set a trait; only an operator's row or `set` does (§7 weight via `has_weight`, not yet read by any caller). An email whose recipients are unknown takes the default `personal` (the operator's own mail). Clear is remembered by content hash, so a path rule or derivation does not put it back while the content is the same; `internal` cannot be cleared under `confidential`. Hooks: `kb_import::import` assigns a page by its bibliography's kinds (`import` when it lists none); a host's `record_research` source of kind `internal`/`person` adds `internal`/`personal` by `host`; `save_claim` derives from the anchors' sources; the inside and the host `record_finding`, `provisional_notes::keep` and `write_merged` derive from the items the text names (and a finding's source evidence) and note `may_rest_on` from the span's given list, which span-start retrieval and `kb_search` record per `<mission>/<role>`. `handling_reconcile` (dry run on a scratch copy of the records; `delphi handling reconcile` is a dry run until the first `--apply`); `set` and `clear` refuse an agent seat and a process without a terminal; doctor check 23. Tests: AC-1 to AC-4 and AC-6 in the engine, AC-5 by the code (no trust decision reads a set) and by behaviour (no private-read mark), a write-path inventory (`WRITES`/`WRITERS`), the clear's one caller, the CLI end to end |

## The desktop app in the release: spec 122 (2026-11-16, approved)

The operator asked on 2026-11-16 for the shortest path to shipping the desktop app as the console is shipped, was shown a two-step plan and its estimate, and answered "do step one and step two, but macos only and only on release".

| Spec | Slice | Content | State |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| 122 | R0 | the spec: `Delphi.app` built by the release workflow on macOS only, in the same tarball as `delphi`; installed by `install.sh` and replaced by `delphi update` at `~/Applications/Delphi.app`, the previous one kept, `--rollback`; Linux, signing, CI and dev builds out of scope | approved 2026-11-16 ("do step one and step two, but macos only and only on release") |
| 122 | R1 | the release workflow: the app's checks and build on the two macOS targets, the tarball, the Homebrew formula | built (pull request open; a gate path, for the operator to merge at a terminal): on the macOS entries only, Node 26.4.0, `npm ci --ignore-scripts` and `npm run check`, the bridge's tests and clippy on the native arm64 entry, `tauri build --target <target> --bundles app --config '{"version":...}'`, an ad-hoc signature over the bundle (`codesign --force --sign -`, then `--verify --strict`: the unsigned build signs only the executable, and a bundle that does not verify is called "damaged" when a browser downloaded it), the version checked in `Info.plist`; `Delphi.app` packed beside `delphi` with `COPYFILE_DISABLE=1`, no link in it and its executable in the listing; the formula keeps it in its prefix on macOS and its caveats give the `ln -sfn` line. **Checked locally:** both targets built with the release's command (15 MB each; arm64 and cross-built x86_64), the seal verifies, the formula parses (`ruby -c`), the workflow parses. **Dry run** of the workflow on this branch (run 44023365725, 2026-11-17): every job passed, the four builds included. The arm64 tarball holds `Delphi.app` (with its seal, no link, no `._` file) beside `delphi` and `delphi-ui`; R2's `install.sh` installed it from a local mirror into a scratch home, where the seal verified, the version read 0.6.3, and the app opened through LaunchServices with no Gatekeeper prompt and was quit. **Not run:** a tagged release |
| 122 | R2 | `install.sh` and `delphi update`: install, replace, keep, roll back; the guide | built and merged (#764): `selfupdate.rs` reads only `<top>/Delphi.app/` entries (regular files and folders with plain names, at most 2,000, the per-file limit; a link, a `..` or any other entry refuses the update before anything is written), writes the bundle in `~/Applications/.delphi-app-new-<pid>` before `delphi-ui` and `delphi` are replaced, then swaps it in and keeps the replaced one as `~/Applications/.delphi-app.prev`; `--rollback` swaps the two back. A release without the app, a link (Homebrew's) or a file at `~/Applications/Delphi.app`, `DELPHI_NO_APP=1`, Linux and every test leave the app alone. `install.sh` installs it at `~/Applications/Delphi.app` (`--no-app`), after the program, refusing an app holding anything but files and folders. **Checked:** unit tests for each case (AC-4, AC-5); the updater's own reader and writer unpacked a tarball made with macOS `tar` and `COPYFILE_DISABLE=1` from a real build of the app into a copy identical to the build; `install.sh` against a local mirror in a scratch home (install, reinstall, a link in the app's place, `--no-app`, an app holding a link). **Not checked:** a real `delphi update` between two published releases with the app (needs R1 and two tags), and `brew install` |

## The Council and the Panel from a host: spec 123 (2026-11-16)

The operator asked on 2026-11-16 for a better Council and Panel experience in the host: seeing activity, getting value from mixed families, and the learning loop. A read-only assessment ranked sixteen changes; the operator kept the Council/Panel split and said "build it". Section 10 of the spec lists the items that are not builds.

| Spec | Slice | Content | State |

A span whose calls all fail ends `Escalated` with the error as its reason (the test found this; it is not an `Err`), so an escalation is treated as an error too: "escalated (family F, endpoint E): <redacted reason>", and "failed (...)" for an `Err`; at most 300 characters of that text (`council::ABSTENTION_MAX`), then " (cut; the whole error is in `delphi logs --mission <id>`)". The whole redacted reason goes to the error log at `error` (slug `council_seat_failed`, context mission, task, role, `provider` = the endpoint, since the log's context keys have no `endpoint`; the log itself caps a message at 1,500 characters). A timeout writes no error-log record (there is no error text). The card's "Who sat" shows the stance text as before (§5 item 3). Not verified: a live seat failure |

Choices: a host's Council has the 3 default seats, so `require_families = 4` is always refused (`seats_unavailable: a Council from a host has 3 seats ...`); `[seats] require_families` is not applied to the daemon Council, only the argument; `spread_families` moves non-architect seats onto unused families (never a critic onto the author's) until the plan seats M, and a family lost between the check and the plan escalates with a `seats_unavailable:` sentence before `council.started`. `council.started` records `require_families`; the daemon card prints "require_families = M: met / NOT met (by the receipts)". Choices: a watch started mid-Council prints at most the last 20 earlier lines; a reason (escalation or error text) is not printed, only the state; console Councils (no `host.council.requested`) are found by `council.started`. Seat text never read into the output (marker test). Not run live against a running daemon; the `budget_tokens` key must match S2's |

NOT built: `/retro` does not list forecasts past their date. | 129 | S1 | the app socket's lockout replaced by a per-peer cost and a flood test (spec 120 §3 item 4 as amended) | built 2026-11-20 (pull request open, not merged; the operator's "go" for wave 1). No lockout: every connection within the limits gets the challenge; a refusal's answer waits, from a peer's (or a claimed name's) second failure, 250 ms doubling to 5 s, and past 20 failures a minute on the socket (`delphi_core::app_refusals`); a valid signature is answered at once. Unproven places: 8 for the socket, 3 per peer (process id, else user id) counting its connections that wait out a refusal; those wait in a pool of their own (16) so a flood cannot fill the places a valid key needs; a connection waiting for its pairing code holds no place of its peer's, and wrong codes typed at the terminal cost no one a wait. `app.refused` writes 20 a minute, then one `reason: aggregated` record with the count. Spec 120 §3 item 4 edited, AC-21 added. Tests: `app_refusals` unit tests (time injected) and `crates/delphi-engine/tests/app_socket.rs` (flood from one process and from rotating process ids under one name, AC-8; per-name cost across processes; per-peer limit; log aggregation; pairing not penalised). Not handled: a flood from more parallel processes than the socket's 8 unproven places can still crowd a knock out (spec 120 §3 item 4: a hostile process of the operator's can always interrupt) |
| 129 | S2 | revoke cancels the key's running requests; a page rebuild cancel handle (spec 120 §2 item 8 and AC-10 as amended) | built 2026-11-20 (pull request open, not merged; S1 #807 merged first; the operator's "go" for waves 1-2). Premise checked: a rebuild had no cancel handle; one is added, a flag (`library::topics::Cancel`) read at the rebuild's gate before the writer's model call and once more before the page file is written; a rebuild past that last check runs to its end and its `app.request` event says `revoked: after_last_check`; the tokens a stopped rebuild spent are journalled. The server, after a revoke or `request` taken away has written the record and closed the sessions, calls a new `Extension::app_requests_cancel`; the engine cancels each unended request of the key (research through `Jobs::cancel`; a request still being admitted at its next step); each writes `app.request` `cancelled`, reason `revoked`, by request id. `app.revoked` and `app.granted` gain `requests_cancelled` (count, ids) and `left_running`. Every admitted request is entered in the tally before a second look at the key's record (refused if the key or `request` is gone), so a request arriving during a revoke is refused or cancelled. An update in flight (`app.update_apply`) is not stopped and is named in `left_running` (work plan §6 question 3, the recommendation). The test `…_is_not_cut_by_a_revocation` was changed to the amended rule. Spec 120 §2 item 8 and AC-10 edited. Tests: race cases held at each step by a test pause (no sleeps), the queue's cancel, the rebuild's checks, the update left running, the socket-level revoke |
